Pre-potential in the A ​ d ​ S 5 × S 5 AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5} Type IIB superspace August 2, 2016 YITP-SB-16-33
Martin Poláček, Warren Siegel

Abstract
We found the pre-potential in the superspace with A ​ d ​ S 5 × S 5 AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5} background. The pre-potential appears as part of the vielbeins, without derivatives. The space-cone gauge destroys the bulk Lorentz covariance, but still preserves boundary Lorentz covariance (projective superspace) S ​ O ​ ( 3 , 1 ) ⊗ S ​ O ​ ( 4 ) SO\,(\,3,\,1\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,4\,) , i.e. symmetries of boundary CFT are manifest.



1
Introduction

In the paper
[
19
]
we obtained the curvature tensor (previously discovered in
[
21
]
) in a manifestly T-dual way. In the paper
[
20
]
we extended the techniques for the case of three dimensional
\mathcal{N}\,=\,2
T-dual extended superspace. There we correctly obtained the pre-potential (as a part of vielbein), structure of linearised dilaton and field equations. The aim of this paper was to look at the full ten dimensional
\mathcal{N}\,=\,2
T-dually extended superspace in the flat and also in
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
background, i.e. IIB string theory expanded around
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
background. The
AdS
was earlier analysed in superspace in papers
[
15
]
,
[
16
]
,
[
17
]
and
[
18
]
. In this paper we discovered the projective (and also the chiral) pre-potential to sit in a certain combination of
H_{S\,\widetilde{S}}
and
H_{D\,\widetilde{D}}
. This was first obtained in the flat case and later generalised for the
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
. We also performed the near horizon limit and derived the equation of motion for the pre-potential in that limit. This limit also picks out the projective pre-potential instead of the chiral pre-potential, even though both pre-potentials are valid bulk solutions. The projective and harmonic superspaces were earlier analysed in
[
13
]
and
[
14
]
.

2
Type II superspace, notation and motivation

2.1
10
dimensional type II superspace

We are closely following paper
[
24
]
. In that paper the superspace was defined by two sets of non-degenerate super-Poincaré covariant derivatives (and their stringy generalisations). For convenience we are repeating the graded commutation relations for the flat space (string) covariant derivatives
\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 4.99998pt\hskip-4.99998pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\nabla$}\hskip-4.99998pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{7.83331pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.6pt\circ$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 4.99998pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 4.99998pt\hskip-4.99998pt\hbox{$\textstyle\nabla$}\hskip-4.99998pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{7.83331pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.6pt\circ$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 4.99998pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 4.1736pt\hskip-4.1736pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\nabla$}\hskip-4.1736pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{5.78334pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.42pt\circ$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 4.1736pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 3.7153pt\hskip-3.7153pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}\hskip-3.7153pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{4.41667pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.3pt\circ$}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.7153pt}}_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}
and symmetry generators
\widetilde{\nabla}_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}
. The multi-index
{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}\,\in\,(\,S,\,D,\,P,\,\mathchar 266,\,\mathchar 262)
. The indices
(\,S,\,D,\,P,\,\mathchar 266,\,\mathchar 262\,)
are multi-indices for (left and right): local Lorentz, supersymmetry, translation, and their dual generators. The whole approach is explicitly explained in
[
24
]
and also in
[
19
]
and in foundational work
[
21
]
and later
[
22
]
. The generators
\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 4.99998pt\hskip-4.99998pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\nabla$}\hskip-4.99998pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{7.83331pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.6pt\circ$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 4.99998pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 4.99998pt\hskip-4.99998pt\hbox{$\textstyle\nabla$}\hskip-4.99998pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{7.83331pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.6pt\circ$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 4.99998pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 4.1736pt\hskip-4.1736pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\nabla$}\hskip-4.1736pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{5.78334pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.42pt\circ$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 4.1736pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 3.7153pt\hskip-3.7153pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}\hskip-3.7153pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{4.41667pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.3pt\circ$}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.7153pt}}_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}
and
\widetilde{\nabla}_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}
satisfy:

\displaystyle{[}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 4.99998pt\hskip-4.99998pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\nabla$}\hskip-4.99998pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{7.83331pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.6pt\circ$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 4.99998pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 4.99998pt\hskip-4.99998pt\hbox{$\textstyle\nabla$}\hskip-4.99998pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{7.83331pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.6pt\circ$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 4.99998pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 4.1736pt\hskip-4.1736pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\nabla$}\hskip-4.1736pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{5.78334pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.42pt\circ$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 4.1736pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 3.7153pt\hskip-3.7153pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}\hskip-3.7153pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{4.41667pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.3pt\circ$}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.7153pt}}_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,{{(1)}},\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 4.99998pt\hskip-4.99998pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\nabla$}\hskip-4.99998pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{7.83331pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.6pt\circ$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 4.99998pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 4.99998pt\hskip-4.99998pt\hbox{$\textstyle\nabla$}\hskip-4.99998pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{7.83331pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.6pt\circ$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 4.99998pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 4.1736pt\hskip-4.1736pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\nabla$}\hskip-4.1736pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{5.78334pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.42pt\circ$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 4.1736pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 3.7153pt\hskip-3.7153pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}\hskip-3.7153pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{4.41667pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.3pt\circ$}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.7153pt}}_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,{{(2)}}\,{\}}

\displaystyle{[}\,S_{\bf{m}\bf{n}}\,{{(1)}},\,S_{\bf{k}\bf{l}}\,{{(2)}}\,{]}

left algebra
\,\rightarrow\,

-
right algebra

\displaystyle{[}\,\mbox{left},\,\mbox{right}\,\}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,0.

As indicated above, the algebra for the right generators is the same up to the overall sign. We can assign the canonical dimensions to the generators:
\mbox{dim}\,(S,\,D,\,P,\,\mathchar 266,\mathchar 262)\,=\,(0,\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}},\,1,\,{\textstyle{3\over 2}},\,2)
. The
S
generators generate the algebra for
SO\,(\,9,1\,)\,\otimes\,SO(\,9,\,1\,)
, i.e. left and right local Lorentz transformations. The
D
generate left and right SUSY transformation and
P
left and right translations. The
\mathchar 266
and
\mathchar 262
are the left and right dual currents (corresponding to
D
and
S
), see also
[
21
]
.

The only nonvanishing terms in the metric and structure constants are (as can be guessed by dimensional analysis):

\eta_{PP},\ \eta_{S\mathchar 262},\ \eta_{D\mathchar 266},;\quad f_{SPP}\ ,\ f_{SS\mathchar 262}\ ,\ f_{DDP}\ ,\ f_{SD\mathchar 266}

(6)

where we have lowered the upper index on
f
with
\eta
to take advantage of its total (graded) antisymmetry, and used “schematic” notation, replacing explicit indices with their type:

{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,(\,_{MN},\,_{\mu},\,_{M},\,^{\mu},\,^{\,MN\,}\,)\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,(\,S,\,D,\,P,\,\mathchar 266,\,\mathchar 262\,)

(7)

Explicitly these are, for the left-handed algebra:

(\eta)_{\bf mn}\,=\,\eta_{\bf mn}\,,\,\ (\eta)_{\bf mn}{}^{\bf pq}\,=\,\delta_{\bf mn}{}^{\bf pq}\,,\,(\eta)_{\bf\sigma}{}^{\bf\rho}\,=\,\delta_{\bf\sigma}^{\bf\rho}\\

(8)

f_{\bf mn}{}^{\bf p\,q}\,=\,-\,\delta_{\bf mn}{}^{\bf pq}\,,\,\ f_{\bf mn\,pq}{}^{\bf rs}\,=\,\eta_{\bf[m[p}\delta_{\bf q]n]}{}^{\bf rs}\,,\,f_{\bf\sigma\rho}{}^{\bf m}\,=\,2\,(\,\gamma^{\bf m}\,)_{\bf\sigma\rho}\,,\,f_{\bf mn\,\sigma}{}^{\bf\rho}\,=\,-\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,(\,\gamma_{\bf mn}\,)_{\bf\sigma}^{\bf\rho}

(9)

To proceed further we introduce the explicit notation for the left and right indices. We will call the left index to be the one indicated in algebra
2
(i.e. without tilde). The right index will be with tilde:
(\mbox{left})\,\equiv\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}\,\equiv\,(S_{\bf{mn}},\,D_{\bf{\mu}},\,P_{\bf{m}},\,\mathchar 266^{\bf{\mu}},\,\mathchar 262^{\bf{mn}})\,\equiv\,(S,\,D,\,P,\,\mathchar 266,\,\mathchar 262)
and
(\mbox{right})\,\equiv\,\widetilde{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\equiv\,(S_{\bf{\widetilde{mn}}},\,D_{\bf{\widetilde{\mu}}},\,P_{\bf{\widetilde{m}}},\,\mathchar 266^{\bf{\widetilde{\mu}}},\,\mathchar 262^{\bf{\widetilde{mn}}})\,\equiv\,(\widetilde{S},\,\widetilde{D},\,\widetilde{P},\,\widetilde{\mathchar 266},\,\widetilde{\mathchar 262})
. (Note, we abuse the notation for indices a bit, compare with general indices definition in (
7
). From the context it should be clear which definition we are using).

2.2
Gamma matrices

The gamma matrices
(\gamma_{\bf{m}})_{\mu\,\nu}
used in the algebra (
2
) are the
16\otimes 16
block gamma matrices from
10
dimensional
32\otimes 32
chiral representation:

\displaystyle\mathchar 256_{\bf{m}}=\begin{pmatrix}0&(\gamma_{\bf{m}})^{\mu\,\nu}\\
(\gamma_{\bf{m}})_{\mu\,\nu}&0\end{pmatrix}

where

\displaystyle\{\,\mathchar 256_{\bf{m}},\,\mathchar 256_{\bf{n}}\,\}\,=\,2\,\eta_{\bf{m}\,\bf{n}}\,\delta.

(10)

Moreover the block gamma matrices satisfy:

\displaystyle(\gamma_{\bf{m}})_{\mu\,\nu}\,=\,(\gamma_{\bf{m}})_{\nu\,\mu}\,\,\,\,\,||\,\,

\displaystyle(\gamma_{\bf{(m}})^{\mu\,\nu}\,(\gamma_{\bf{\,n)}})_{\nu\,\sigma}\,=\,2\,\eta_{\bf{m\,n}}\,\delta_{\sigma}^{\mu}\,\,\,\,\,||\,\,

\displaystyle(\gamma_{\bf{m}})_{(\mu\,\nu}(\gamma^{\bf{m}})_{\sigma)\,\lambda}\,=\,0

(11)

The IIB fermion generators (in algebra
2
) are described by
16\,\oplus\,16
chiral fermion generators (for left and right generators) with same
10
dimensional chirality. For the future use we need to look closer at the structure of the matrices
(\gamma_{\bf{m}})_{\mu\nu}
from equation (
10
). The equation (
10
) gamma matrices could be constructed from
SO\,(\,9\,)
gamma matrices or equivalently from
SO\,(\,8\,)
gamma matrices and the chirality matrix.
We can go one step down and construct the
SO\,(\,8\,)
gamma matrices from
SO\,(\,6\,)
gamma matrices. For the
SO\,(\,6\,)
gamma matrices we use the Majorana representation of those matrices (they are purely imaginary). Thus we can get the Majorana - Weyl representation of the
SO\,(\,8\,)
gamma matrices.

For the future reference we will define the following
16\,\otimes\,16
matrix
{\widetilde{\mathchar 256}}_{5}
:

\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5}\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,\gamma_{{\bf{10}}}\,\prod\limits_{{\bf{m}}\,=\,1}^{4}\,\gamma_{{\bf{m}}}

(12)

where
\gamma_{{\bf{m}}}
are block gamma matrices from (
10
).

2.3
Space - cone basis and indices

Because in the future we will use the space - cone (and light - cone) basis we introduce it for the gamma matrices we constructed in previous subsection. We first notice that the block gamma matrices
\gamma_{{\bf{m}}}
in equation (
10
) could have either upper indices
(\gamma_{{\bf{m}}})^{\mu\,\nu}
or lower indices
(\gamma_{{\bf{m}}})_{\mu\,\nu}
. From the construction it follows that those matrices are equal up to the sign.

In the equation (
10
) let us further divide the (either upper or lower)
16
dimensional index
\mu
to
8\,\oplus\,8
pieces (they are the
SO\,(\,8\,)
chiral indices), thus we introduce
\mu\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,(\,\mu,\,\mu^{\prime}\,)
. In another words we want to look how the block
\gamma_{{\bf{m}}}
matrices look in the
SO\,(\,8\,)
(Majorana - Weyl) basis. Furthermore we introduce the following space - cone combinations of the equation (
10
) block gamma matrices:

\displaystyle(\gamma_{{\bf{+}}})_{\mu\,\nu}\,=\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,(\,\gamma_{{\bf{10}}}\,+\,\gamma_{{\bf{9}}}\,)_{\mu\,\nu}\,=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&\delta_{\mu^{\prime}\,\nu^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}\,\,\,||\,\,\,(\gamma_{{\bf{-}}})_{\mu\,\nu}\,=\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,(\,\gamma_{{\bf{10}}}\,-\,\gamma_{{\bf{9}}}\,)_{\mu\,\nu}\,=\begin{pmatrix}\delta_{\mu\,\nu}&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}

(13)

\displaystyle(\gamma_{{\bf{+}}})^{\mu\,\nu}\,=\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,(\,\gamma_{{\bf{10}}}\,+\,\gamma_{{\bf{9}}}\,)^{\mu\,\nu}\,=\begin{pmatrix}\delta^{\mu\,\nu}&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\,\,\,||\,\,\,(\gamma_{{\bf{-}}})^{\mu\,\nu}\,=\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,(\,\gamma_{{\bf{10}}}\,-\,\gamma_{{\bf{9}}}\,)^{\mu\,\nu}\,=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&\delta^{\mu^{\prime}\,\nu^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}

For the convenience we also write the remaining gamma matrices using the
SO\,(\,8\,)
indices:

\displaystyle(\gamma_{\,{\bf{i}}})_{\mu\,\nu}\,=\,\begin{pmatrix}0&(\,\widetilde{\gamma}_{\,{\bf{i}}}\,)_{\mu\,\nu^{\prime}}\\
(\,\widetilde{\gamma}_{\,{\bf{i}}}\,)_{\mu^{\prime}\,\nu}&0\end{pmatrix}\,\,\,||\,\,\,(\gamma_{\,{\bf{i}}})^{\mu\,\nu}\,=\,\begin{pmatrix}0&(\,\widetilde{\gamma}_{\,{\bf{i}}}\,)^{\mu\,\nu^{\prime}}\\
(\,\widetilde{\gamma}_{\,{\bf{i}}}\,)^{\mu^{\prime}\,\nu}&0\end{pmatrix}

(14)

where the
\widetilde{\gamma}_{\,\bf{i}}
are the
SO\,(\,8\,)
gamma matrices.

In the above introduced
8\,\oplus\,8
basis the (
12
) looks like
\sigma_{3}\,\otimes\,{\mathds{1}}
where
{\mathds{1}}
is the
8\,\otimes\,8
unit matrix and
\sigma_{3}
is the Pauli matrix.

3
The
AdS
background in the T-dually extended superspace

3.1
Short review of the theory in curved background

In the usual treatment of the theory (of T-dually extended superspaces) the curved background is introduced via vielbeins
E_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}}}{}^{{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}}}(X^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}}})
, see papers
[
21
]
,
[
19
]
,
[
20
]
:

\mathchar 261_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}}}{\mbox{\footnotesize(1)}}\,=\,E_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}}}{}^{{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}}}(X^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}}})\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 4.99998pt\hskip-4.99998pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\nabla$}\hskip-4.99998pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{7.83331pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.6pt\circ$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 4.99998pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 4.99998pt\hskip-4.99998pt\hbox{$\textstyle\nabla$}\hskip-4.99998pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{7.83331pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.6pt\circ$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 4.99998pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 4.1736pt\hskip-4.1736pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\nabla$}\hskip-4.1736pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{5.78334pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.42pt\circ$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 4.1736pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 3.7153pt\hskip-3.7153pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}\hskip-3.7153pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{4.41667pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.3pt\circ$}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.7153pt}}_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}

(15)

where
\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 4.99998pt\hskip-4.99998pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\nabla$}\hskip-4.99998pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{7.83331pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.6pt\circ$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 4.99998pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 4.99998pt\hskip-4.99998pt\hbox{$\textstyle\nabla$}\hskip-4.99998pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{7.83331pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.6pt\circ$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 4.99998pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 4.1736pt\hskip-4.1736pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\nabla$}\hskip-4.1736pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{5.78334pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.42pt\circ$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 4.1736pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 3.7153pt\hskip-3.7153pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla$}\hskip-3.7153pt\hskip-1.84029pt\raisebox{4.41667pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.3pt\circ$}\hskip-1.84029pt\hskip 3.7153pt}}_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}
are generators of the flat algebra (
2
). The affine Lie algebra for the
\mathchar 261_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}}}{\mbox{\footnotesize(1)}}\,
can be written as:

\begin{array}[]{cccccc}{[}\mathchar 261_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}}}{\mbox{\footnotesize(1)}},\mathchar 261_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}}}{\mbox{\footnotesize(2)}}{]}\,\equiv\,-i\eta_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}}{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}}}\,\delta^{\prime}\,(\,{\mbox{\footnotesize{2}}}\,-\,{\mbox{\footnotesize 1}}\,)-iT_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}}{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}}}{}^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{E}$}}}}}}}\mathchar 261_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{E}$}}}}}}}\,\delta\,(\,{\mbox{\footnotesize{2}}}\,-\,{\mbox{\footnotesize 1}}\,)\end{array}

(16)

where
T_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}}{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}}}{}^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{E}$}}}}}}}
is a (super)stringy generalisation of torsion, see
[
19
]
:

T_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}}{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}}}{}^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{E}$}}}}}}}=E_{[{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}}}{}^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}}(D_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}}E_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}})}{}^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}}})E^{-1}_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}}}{}^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{E}$}}}}}}}+{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\eta^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{E}$}}}}}}{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{D}$}}}}}}}E_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{D}$}}}}}}}{}^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}}(D_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}}E_{[{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}}|}{}^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}}})E^{-1}_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}}}{}^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{F}$}}}}}}}\eta_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{F}$}}}}}}|{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}})}+E_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}}}{}^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}}E_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}}}{}^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}}}E^{-1}_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{P}$}}}}}}}{}^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{E}$}}}}}}}f_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}}}{}^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{P}$}}}}}}}

(17)

where
[\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,|\,|\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}\,)
indicates graded anti-symmetrization in only those indices. By
D_{{\scriptsize\mathcal{M}}}
in (
17
) and in the whole text we mean the group covariant derivatives of the (non-affine) part of algebra (
2
):
[\,D_{{\scriptsize\mathcal{M}}},\,D_{{\scriptsize\mathcal{N}}}\,\}\,=\,i\,f_{{\scriptsize\mathcal{M}}\,{\scriptsize\mathcal{N}}}{}^{{\scriptsize\mathcal{E}}}\,D_{{\scriptsize\mathcal{E}}}
.

Note that the (super)Jacobi identities imply the total graded antisymmetry of the torsion, just as for the structure constants. Torsion (
17
) can be identified with that of “ordinary” curved-space covariant derivatives by use of the strong constraint, as explained in
[
19
,
21
]
.

We can set the coefficient of the Schwinger term to be the metric
\eta
; the vielbein is forced to obey the orthogonality constraints:

E_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}}}{}^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}}\eta_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}}}\,E_{\,{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}}}{}^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}}}\,\equiv\,\eta_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}}{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}}}

(18)

This choice does not affect the physics, and simplifies many of the expressions. For example, it implies the total graded antisymmetry of the torsion, when the upper index is implicitly lowered with
\eta
:

T_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A\,B\,C}$}}}}}\,=\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}E_{[\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,|}{}^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}(D_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}E_{|\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}{}^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}})E_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}\,)\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,+\,E_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}{}^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}E_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}{}^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}E_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}{}^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{P}$}}}}}f_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M\,N\,P}$}}}}}

(19)

where we have used
E^{-1}_{\,\,\,{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}}{}^{\,{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}}}\,=\,\eta^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A\ B}$}}}}}}}\eta_{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M\ N}$}}}}}}}E_{\,{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}}}{}^{{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}}}
. (Also note that in the first term the graded anti-symmetrization can be written as a cyclic sum without the
1/2
, since it is already graded antisymmetric in the last two indices.) Thus, because of orthogonality, the vielbein is like (the exponential of) a super
2
-form, while the torsion is a super
3
-form; similarly, the Bianchi identities are a super
4
-form.

To solve the theory (in terms of pre-potential, to get physical fields and equation of motion) orthogonality condition (
18
) has to be solved explicitly (or at least at linearised level). Moreover there is a huge gauge group invariance that should be fixed:

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathchar 259}\,\mathchar 261_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}\,=\,[\,-\,i\,\mathchar 259,\,\mathchar 261_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}\,\}

\displaystyle\mbox{where}\,\,\mathchar 259\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,\int\,d1\,\lambda^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,(\,X\,)\,D_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}

(20)

At the top of the orthogonality condition and the gauge invariance, we should include the torsion constraints. The torsion constraints are additional constraints on vielbein. They are imposed by putting some of the torsions in (
19
) to zero. Of course, not all torsions in (
19
) are zero. The relevant torsion constraints have been carefully analysed in
[
24
]
. All possible constraints on torsions are coming from curved space version of the
{\bf{A\,B\,C\,D}}
(first class) constraints:
{\bf{A}}
Virasoro, (string world-sheet) diffeomorphism constraints,
B
and
C
and
{\bf{D}}
are the first class fermionic
\kappa
symmetry constraints, for further details see
[
8
]
,
[
9
]
,
[
10
]
,
[
11
]
,
[
12
]
and
[
24
]
. The rule of thumb is that at least the torsions of negative (10 dimensional) engineering dimension should be zero.

We will not try to solve the full nonlinear version of the theory. We linearise the theory around some background. In previous papers
[
19
]
,
[
20
]
we linearised around the flat background. In this paper however we linearise the theory around the
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
background solution of the classical supergravity (reformulated in the language of the doubled algebra).

After the linearisation we rewrite the orthogonality constraints (
18
) and torsions (
19
) using the vielbein expansion
E_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}{}^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{D}$}}}}}\,=\,\delta_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}{}^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{D}$}}}}}\,+\,E^{{\scriptsize(1)}}{}_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}{}^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{D}$}}}}}\,+\,{\mathcal{O}}\,(\,E^{{\scriptsize(2)}}\,)
. Let us for simplicity rename the first fluctuation
E^{{\scriptsize(1)}}{}_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}{}^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{D}$}}}}}\,\equiv\,H_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}{}^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{D}$}}}}}
. Then the equation (
18
) is just statement that:
H_{(\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}}{}^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{D}$}}}}}\,\eta_{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{\,}$}}}}D\,|\,E}\,]}\,\equiv\,H_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{(}$}}}}\,C\,E\,]}\,=\,0
and the structure of linearised torsion (
19
):

\displaystyle T_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}\,+\,T^{{\scriptsize(1)}}{}_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}\,+\,{\mathcal{O}}\,(\,T^{{\scriptsize{(2)}}}\,)

(21)

where

\displaystyle T^{{\scriptsize{(1)}}}{}_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}\,\equiv\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,D_{[{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}\,H_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}\,)}\,+\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,H_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{[}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,}{}^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,f_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}\,)\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}

3.2
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
background

In the expansion (
21
) we need to have the concrete structure constants
f_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{\,}$}}}}A\,B\,C}
(i.e. vacuum values of torsions). We are interested in solving the theory (at least identifying the pre-potential) around this
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
background. The relevant structure constants for the T-dually extended superspace in the context of the
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
background were analysed in the last section of the paper
[
24
]
. We are repeating them here for the convenience. In the next section we will embed this
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
version of T-dual algebra (see
[
24
]
) to a certain larger algebra that will be actually used in computations. The relevant non-vanishing
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
torsions from
[
24
]
are:

\displaystyle\mbox{dim $0$}:\,\,

\displaystyle T_{S\,S\,\mathchar 262}\,=\,f_{S\,S\,\mathchar 262}\,\,\,||\,\,\,T_{S\,D\,\mathchar 266}\,=\,f_{S\,D\,\mathchar 266}\,\,\,||\,\,\,T_{S\,P\,P}\,=\,f_{S\,P\,P}\,\,\,||\,\,\,T_{D\,D\,P}\,=\,f_{D\,D\,P}

\displaystyle\mbox{dim $1$}:\,\,

\displaystyle T_{D\,\widetilde{D}\,\mathchar 262}\,=\,R_{D\,\widetilde{D}\,\mathchar 262}\,\,\,||\,\,\,T_{P\,\widetilde{D}\,\mathchar 266}\,=\,R_{P\,\widetilde{D}\,\mathchar 266}

\displaystyle\mbox{dim $2$}:\,\,

\displaystyle T_{\mathchar 266\,\mathchar 266\,P}\,=\,R_{\mathchar 266\,\mathchar 266\,P}\,\,\,||\,\,\,T_{\mathchar 266\,\mathchar 266\,P}\,=\,R_{\mathchar 266\,\mathchar 266\,\widetilde{P}}\,\,\,||\,\,\,T_{P\,\widetilde{P}\,\mathchar 262}\,=\,R_{P\,\widetilde{P}\,\mathchar 262}

\displaystyle\mbox{dim $3$}:\,\,

\displaystyle T_{\mathchar 266\,\widetilde{\mathchar 266}\,\mathchar 262}\,=\,R_{\mathchar 266\,\widetilde{\mathchar 266}\,\mathchar 262}

(22)

note the left and right index notation introduced below (
9
).

The
f_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}
in (
3.2
) are usual flat superspace structure constants for the (flat) T-dually extended superspace with the (common) local Lorentz group
SO\,(\,5\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,4,\,1\,)
. The nontrivial curvatures from table (
3.2
):
\,R_{D\,\widetilde{D}\,\mathchar 262}\,
,
\,R_{P\,\widetilde{D}\,\mathchar 266}\,\dots
are defined using the dimension
1
torsion
T_{P\,\widetilde{D}\,\mathchar 266}
. The
T_{P\,\widetilde{D}\,\mathchar 266}\,\equiv\,T_{{\bf a}\,\widetilde{\alpha}}{}^{\beta}\,=\,\gamma_{{\bf a}\,\,\,{\alpha}\,{\sigma}}\,F^{\beta\,\widetilde{\sigma}}
, where the R-R field strength
F_{\mathchar 266\,\widetilde{\mathchar 266}}\,\equiv\,F^{\alpha\,\widetilde{\beta}}\,=\,{\textstyle{1\over r_{AdS}}}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})^{\alpha\,\beta}
. Note that the
\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5}
was defined in (
12
) and the new parameter
r_{AdS}
is the
AdS_{5}
space radius (note,
r_{AdS}\,=\,r_{S}
i.e. the radius of
S_{5}
is the same of
AdS_{5}
, so that Ricci scalar
R\,=\,0
). More specifically some of the table (
3.2
) curvatures:

\displaystyle\mbox{dim $1$}:\,\,

\displaystyle R_{D\,\widetilde{D}\,\mathchar 262}\,\equiv\,R_{\alpha\,\widetilde{\beta}}{}^{\,\bf a\,b}\,=\,T_{\widetilde{\beta}}{}^{\,\sigma\,[{\bf a}}\,\gamma^{{\bf b}\,]}{}_{\alpha\,\sigma}

(23)

\displaystyle\mbox{dim $1$}:\,\,

\displaystyle R_{P\,\widetilde{D}\,\mathchar 266}\,\equiv\,R_{{\bf a}\,\widetilde{\alpha}}{}^{\beta}\,=\,T_{{\bf a}\,\widetilde{\alpha}}{}^{\beta}\,=\,\gamma_{{\bf a}\,\,\,{\alpha}\,{\sigma}}\,F^{\beta\,\widetilde{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\mbox{dim $2$}:\,\,

\displaystyle R_{P\,\widetilde{P}\,\mathchar 262}\,\equiv\,R_{{\bf a}\,\widetilde{{\bf b}}}{}^{\bf cd}\,\propto\,\,\,T_{\widetilde{{\bf b}}\,\alpha\,}{}^{\widetilde{\beta}}\,R_{\widetilde{\beta}\,\gamma\,}{}^{\bf cd}\,\gamma_{\bf a}{}^{\alpha\,\gamma}

\displaystyle\mbox{dim $3$}:\,\,

\displaystyle R_{\mathchar 266\,\widetilde{\mathchar 266}\,\mathchar 262}\,\equiv\,R^{\alpha\,\widetilde{\beta}}{}^{\,\,\,\bf{ab}}\,\propto\,\,\,\,(\,T^{\widetilde{\bf{d}}\,\,\widetilde{\beta}}{}_{\sigma}\,R_{\widetilde{\bf{d}}\,{\bf{e}}}{}^{\bf{ab}}\,+\,T_{\bf{e}}{}^{\widetilde{\beta}\,\nu}\,R_{\widetilde{\sigma}\,\nu}{}^{\bf{ab}})\,\gamma^{\bf{e}}{}^{\,\,\sigma\,\alpha}

where the dim
2
curvature is proportional with the constant
2^{-\,{\textstyle{D\over 2}}\,+\,1}
and the dim
3
curvature is proportional with a constant
D
(where
D
is
10
in our case).

All the other curvatures (i.e. torsions) in (
23
) are obtained using the appropriate Bianchi identities (one can obtain basically all curvatures from
T_{P\,\widetilde{D}\,\mathchar 266}
using Bianchi identities). We note that the torsions (
3.2
) and curvatures (
23
) are consistent with torsions and curvatures given in the
[
6
]
.

3.3
Extended
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
T-dual algebra

To identify the pre-potential in the generalised vielbeins, i.e. solving the spectrum of the theory (on linearised level) we want to proceed as described in earlier papers
[
24
]
,
[
19
]
,
[
20
]
. There the vielbeins were introduced as in (
15
) and linearised as above equation (
21
). This procedure means the expansion of generally curved superspace around some (in those papers just a flat) background. Moreover the gauge invariance was completely fixed (in referenced papers the covariant gauge was considered) and after that the pre-potential was identified as a part of vielbein (acting on by derivatives, the physical spectrum is produced).

Here we want to proceed in similar way. We want to introduce the vielbeins and linearise the theory around the
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
background. We have tried to use solely the algebra described in the previous sub-section, i.e. to take the algebra (
3.2
) and introduce the vielbeins, gauge fix and linearise. Even though we still believe that the pre-potential is sitting in that theory in some combination of vielbeins, it was not easy to identify it. The reason was that to identify the pre-potential we need to find a scalar contraction of some linear combination of vielbeins that is anihilated by the
D_{v}
and
D_{\bar{v}}
operators. The
D_{v}
and
D_{\bar{v}}
are certain combinations of
D_{\alpha^{{}^{\prime}}}
and
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{{}^{\prime}}}
(see the index notation in (
7
) and above (
13
), i.e. they are a particular chiral part of the
SO\,(\,8\,)
chiral decomposition of the
16
SUSY translations
D_{\alpha}
defined at the beginning, see (
2
) and section above (
13
)).

Because the metric is in
H_{P\,\widetilde{P}}
vielbein, we expected the pre-potential to be (at least a part of it) in
\mbox{Tr}\,H_{P\,\widetilde{P}}
. The problem with
\mbox{Tr}\,H_{P\,\widetilde{P}}
is that it already has dimension
0
. To show that it is annihilated by
D_{v}
and
D_{\bar{v}}
operators we would need to use torsion constraints of dimension
{\textstyle{1\over 2}}
. Moreover we also know that the pre-potential has to be annihilated by the suitably defined
{\mathcal{P}}_{+}
operator (
{\mathcal{P}}_{+}\,\propto\,(\,{P_{+}}\,+\,{P_{\widetilde{+}}}\,)
where
{P_{+}}\,\equiv\,D_{P_{+}}
and
{P_{\widetilde{+}}}\,\equiv\,D_{P_{\widetilde{+}}}
), in a ”light cone” basis introduced in (
13
) and in the ”near horizon limit” (defined later)). The high dimensionality of
\mbox{Tr}\,H_{P\,\widetilde{P}}
then requires to use at least dimension
1
torsion constraints to prove that
{\mathcal{P}}_{+}
vanishes (in the near horizon limit). That seemed to be problematic to analyse in the theory based just on the algebra (
3.2
) and (
23
) because of the degauging procedure. The degauging appears since the theory coming from algebras (
3.2
) and (
23
) is really coming from the full
SO\,(\,10\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,10\,)
so there are some missing Lorentz connections. By simple dimensional analysis its evident that the missing Lorentz connections are first appearing at dimension
1
(for example the appearance of the full
H_{P\,S}
in the dim
1
torsion
T_{P\,P\,P}\,\propto\,\dots\,+\,H_{[\,P|\,S}\,f_{P\,P]\,\mathchar 262}\,+\,\dots
).

For that reason we extended the algebra (
3.2
) to include the original (Wick rotated) local Lorentz group
SO\,(\,10\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,10\,)
. All the other structure constants and curvatures in (
23
) and (
3.2
) stay the same. Except now we have separate left local Lorentz
S_{\bf{ab}}
generator together with the right local Lorentz generator
S_{\widetilde{\bf{ab}}}
, where
{\bf{a}}\,\in\,\{\,1,\,\dots\,10\}
. The common (Wick rotated)
SO\,(\,5\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,5\,)
Lorentz group of original
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
algebra (
3.2
) is then the subgroup in the diagonal
SO\,(\,10\,)
subgroup of
SO\,(\,10\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,10\,)
. The extension procedure can be viewed from the different picture. We could start with the full
10
dimensional string superspace as introduced in
[
21
]
and
[
23
]
. Then introduce the curved version of that space via vielbeins and then linearise around some background as described earlier. The extension of (
3.2
) and (
23
) to full
SO\,(\,10\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,10\,)
is then just a choice of some particular background that is consistent with the original
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
. This has an advantage that now we have a natural place where to put the troubling (part) of the pre-potential
\mbox{Tr}\,H_{P\,\widetilde{P}}
. Because of the additional
S_{\bf{ab}}
and
S_{\widetilde{\bf{ab}}}
we can have
\mbox{Tr}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,\equiv\,\mbox{Tr}\,H_{S\,\widetilde{S}}
instead of the
\mbox{Tr}\,H_{P\,\widetilde{P}}
. The
\mbox{Tr}\,H_{P\,\widetilde{P}}\,\equiv\,H_{{\bf{a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{b}}}
is related to
\mbox{Tr}\,H_{S\,\widetilde{S}}\,\equiv\,\mbox{Tr}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
by an action of
D_{P_{-}}\,\equiv\,P_{-}
and
D_{P_{\widetilde{-}}}\,\equiv\,P_{\widetilde{-}}
(they are both invertible operators). The vielbein
H_{S\,\widetilde{S}}
is of the dimension
-\,2
and so there is no need to use the higher dimensional torsion constraints. Moreover in the full
SO\,(\,10\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,10\,)
theory we do not have to do the degauging procedure.

This extension comes with the cost. The mixed pieces of the
AdS
algebra (
23
) are breaking the explicit
SO\,(\,10\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,10\,)
invariance (they are not the
SO\,(\,10\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,10\,)
invariant tensors). We still have present the full
D_{S}\,\equiv\,S
generators. Those derivatives could hit the (non-invariant) curvatures. The solution of this is to keep the explicit mixed curvature dependence (as generic mixed curvatures) till the
S
derivatives are not being explicitly evaluated. We will describe this procedure in detail later.

4
Gauge fixing

We want to fix the space-cone gauge (T-dual super space-cone gauge) for the first fluctuation
H_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}
, i.e. like in the usual light-cone we have
D_{P_{-}}\,\equiv\,P_{-}
operator invertible, now we have
P_{-}
and
P_{\widetilde{-}}
invertible (where
D_{P_{\widetilde{-}}}\,\equiv\,P_{\widetilde{-}}
).

First we look at the gauge variation (
20
) more closely and at the linearised level:

\delta_{\mathchar 259}\,H_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}\,=\,D_{[{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}\,\lambda_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}\,)}\,+\,f_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}{}^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}\,\lambda_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}

(24)

In the light-cone gauge we in general pick a vielbein with an
P_{-}
or
P_{\widetilde{-}}
index, put that vielbein to zero. In order to maintain that gauge we need to fix the particular gauge parameter. For simplicity we call
P_{-}\,\equiv\,-
and
P_{\widetilde{-}}\,\equiv\,\widetilde{-}
then:

\displaystyle H_{-\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}

\displaystyle\,=\,0\,\,\,\Rightarrow\,\,\,\delta_{\mathchar 259}\,H_{-\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}\,=\,0\,\,\,\Rightarrow\,\,\,P_{-}\,\lambda_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}\,-\,D_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}\,\lambda_{-}\,+f_{-\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}{}^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}\,\lambda_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}\,=\,0

(25)

then

\displaystyle\,\,\lambda_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}\,=\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{-}}}\,(\,D_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}\,\lambda_{-}\,+\,f_{-\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}{}^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}\,\lambda_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{C}$}}}}}\,)

Note that there are more possibilities to fix the particular gauge parameters
\lambda_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}
. To fix
\lambda_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}
we could also put
H_{\widetilde{-}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}\,=\,0
and use the invertibility of
P_{\widetilde{-}}
. Of course we can not fix some gauge parameter twice. We have to decide which vielbeins we are going to fix in this “double” light-cone gauge.

We picked the approach where we used the mixed vielbeins to vanish by the double light-cone gauge fixing, i.e. we put
H_{\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}\,=\,0
for
{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,\in\{\,S,\,D,\,P,\,\mathchar 266,\,\mathchar 262\,\}\,\equiv\,\mbox{left part of algebra}
. Together with
H_{-\,\widetilde{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}}\,=\,0
for
\widetilde{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}\,\in\{\,\widetilde{S},\,\widetilde{D},\,\widetilde{P},\,\widetilde{\mathchar 266},\,\widetilde{\mathchar 262}{}\,\}\,\equiv\,\mbox{right part of algebra}
. By that choice we fully fix the gauge parameters
\lambda_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}
and
\lambda_{\widetilde{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}}
in terms of
\lambda_{-}
. That parameter can be fixed by the gauge invariance of the gauge invariance.

The motivation for the previously described mixed left right light-cone gauge fixing came from the flat space (just the extended
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
space with
r_{AdS}\,\rightarrow\,\infty
, i.e. the flat
SO\,(\,10\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,10\,)
T-dual superspace). After picking this type of the light-cone gauge the mixed torsion constraints of the type
T_{\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}\,=\,0
are as algebraic as possible:

\displaystyle T_{\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{\widetilde{-}}\,H_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}\,+D_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}\,H_{\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}\,+D_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}\,H_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}\,\widetilde{-}}\,+H_{\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}f_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,+H_{[{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{\,}$}}}}A\,|\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}f_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}\,)\,\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

(26)

\displaystyle T_{\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{\widetilde{-}}\,H_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}\,=\,0\,\,\,\Rightarrow\,\,\,H_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}\,=\,0\,\,\mbox{for}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}},\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}\,\,:\,\,T_{\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}\,=\,0

(27)

where we used our mixed light-cone gauge and
r_{AdS}\,\rightarrow\,\infty
of extended algebra in (
27
).

The same as in (
26
) and (
27
) holds if one fully swaps left and right indices. For finite
r_{AdS}
we can have the mixed structure constants nonzero (i.e.
f_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}\,\widetilde{-}}\,\neq\,0
) and so we would have a right hand side in (
27
). Note also that there could be the contribution from
S
derivatives hitting the mixed structure constants. Even though the right hand side in (
27
) is not generally vanishing for finite
r_{AdS}
we found that the mixed left-right light-cone gauge is still useful in the
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
case.

5
Torsion constraints

5.1
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
curvatures and
D_{S}
derivatives

As we noted in the introduction section. Because we have enhanced our superspace, we have to take special care when the local Lorentz derivatives
D_{S}\,\equiv\,S
are hitting the mixed curvatures (
23
). This problem arises because the curvatures in (
23
) are not full
SO\,(\,10\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,10\,)
invariant. The solution is to keep the non-invariant torsions (
23
) generic and explicitly act by the
D_{S}\,\equiv\,S
derivatives on those torsions. Only after this explicit
S
action we can evaluate those torsions (or curvatures) and be fixed as in (
23
).

Let’s take an example, from the equation (
26
) we can see that in the
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
case in the mixed light-cone gauge the
H_{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}}
is determined as:

\displaystyle H_{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}}\,=\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,H_{[\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}|\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}})\,{\widetilde{-}}\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}}

(28)

In many instances in this paper we use similar relation as in (
28
) to fix some particular vielbein in terms of another vielbeins. If all structure constants
f
would be
SO\,(\,10\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,10\,)
invariant tensors then there is not a problem and we can treat the
f
structure constants as genuine constants also with respect to the
S
derivatives. In our case however the mixed
f
structure constants (that we call also the curvatures) in (
23
) explicitly break the
SO\,(\,10\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,10\,)
local Lorentz invariance down to the
SO\,(\,5\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,5\,)
local Lorentz (as it should be in the
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
case). One possibility is to restrict our superspace local Lorentz invariance (the
S
derivatives) to
SO\,(\,5\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,5\,)
. Then we would return back to the
PSU\,(\,2,2\,|\,4\,)
that we wanted to avoid in the first place (in order to have
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
instead of
H_{{\bf{a}}\,{\widetilde{\bf{b}}}}
). The alternative, that we picked, is to work with the full
SO\,(\,10\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,10\,)
local Lorentz group. But then the structure constants that are breaking that invariance are not invariant tensors and so the action of those
S
derivatives on the mixed structure constants has to be accounted for. So we should keep the mixed
f
structure constants and when needed explicitly act by the
S
derivatives on them. We will evaluate them as the very last step in our calculations. Let’s look at the example in (
28
) and look at the action of
{S}_{\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}
, where
{\bf{a}}\,\in\,\{\,1\,\dots\,8\}
:

\displaystyle S_{\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}\,H_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}\,{\Big(}\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,H_{[\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}|\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,{\Big)}\,f_{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}})\,{\widetilde{-}}\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}}\,+\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,H_{[\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}|\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,{\Big(}\,S_{\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}\,f_{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}})\,{\widetilde{-}}\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}}\,{\Big)}

(29)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}\,{\Big(}\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,H_{[\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}|\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,{\Big)}\,f_{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}})\,{\widetilde{-}}\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}}\,+\,\eta_{\bf{+-}}\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,H_{[\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}|\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}})\,{\widetilde{\bf{a}}}\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}}

We will evaluate the
f_{{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}\,{\widetilde{\bf{a}}}\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}}
in the second equation just after all the (possibly future)
S
derivatives have already acted. We also should bear in mind that whenever we are acting by the
S
derivative on some vielbein (that is determined by another vielbeins) there might be the above described issue. The second term can (and it will) nontrivially contribute to our calculations.

5.2
Torsion constraints and
H_{S\,S}
vielbein

The torsion constraints are (mainly) given by the curved version of the
{\bf{A\,B\,C\,D}}
(first class) constraints, see
[
8
]
and
[
24
]
. There are further constraints called
\widetilde{T}_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}\,=\,0
coming from requirement of partial integration in the presence of the dilaton measure, see
[
21
]
, and
[
20
]
. There is also a strong constraint: on every field in the double field theory one has to require
D^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}\,D_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}\,=\,0
. There are also a dimensional reduction constraints, as we see later.

Our aim is to analyse the necessary constraints consistent with the above constraints by which we can identify the pre-potential. Following the analysis given in
[
7
]
, we identify the pre-potential as a scalar super-field (given by some super-trace of possibly a combination of vielbeins), that is annihilated by certain combination of the
D_{\nu^{\prime}}
and
D_{\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}}
. The precise combination of
D_{\nu^{\prime}}
and
D_{\widetilde{\nu}^{\prime}}
is also going to be determined from the constraints.

As usual, we start to eliminate the lowest dimensional vielbeins. The vielbeins of the lowest dimension are
H_{S\,S},\,\,H_{S\,\widetilde{S}},\,H_{\widetilde{S}\,\widetilde{S}}
. They are of the dimension
-\,2
(we mean the ten dimensional dimension). Using equations (
26
) and (
27
) for indices
{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,=\,S
and
{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}\,=\,S
(also after change
\mbox{left}\,\leftrightarrow\,\mbox{right}
) we immediately get that
H_{S\,S}\,=\,0\,=\,H_{\widetilde{S}\,\widetilde{S}}
. Note, that even in the extended
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
superspace the structure constant
f_{S\,\widetilde{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}\,=\,0\,\rightarrow\,f_{S\,\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}\,=\,0
.

We mention an important observation that will help us simplify future calculations. As we saw in previous sub-chapter, we should keep the mixed structure constants generic and evaluate them at the end. Note however, that the mixed structure constants with the
S
indices are always zero (like the one we considered here:
f_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{S}$}}}}\widetilde{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}}
). The action of
S
derivatives on them results in the mixed structure constants again with the
S
index and such are zero after the evaluation. So specifically, we can evaluate the mixed structure constants with the
S
indices to zero even before acting by
S
derivatives on them.

The mixed vielbein
H_{S\,\widetilde{S}}
is not all zero and the claim is that the part of the pre-potential is in this particular vielbein. To see which parts are possibly nonzero we rewrite the
H_{S\,\widetilde{S}}
in the double light-cone components:

\displaystyle H_{S\,\widetilde{S}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\{\,H_{\bf{+a}\,\widetilde{\bf{+c}}},\,H_{\bf{+a}\,\widetilde{\bf{-b}}},\,H_{{\bf{-a}}{\widetilde{\bf{-b}}}},\,H_{\bf{+a}\,\widetilde{\bf{bc}}},\,H_{\bf{-a}\,\widetilde{\bf{bc}}},\,H_{\bf{ab}\,\widetilde{\bf{cd}}},\,H_{+-\,\widetilde{+-}},\,H_{\bf{+-}\,\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}

(30)

\displaystyle H_{\bf{+-}\,\widetilde{\bf{-a}}},\,H_{\bf{+-}\,\widetilde{\bf{ab}}}\}\,\oplus\,\,\mbox{swap}

where we might swap left index with the right index in (
30
). Also note that in all previous we have
{\bf{a}}\,\in\,\{\,{{1}},\dots,\,{{8}}\,\}
. We remind that
P_{+}\,\equiv\,{\bf{+}}\,\propto\,{{10}\,+\,{{9}}}
and
P_{-}\,\equiv\,{\bf{-}}\,\propto\,{{10}\,-\,{{9}}}
.

We want to use analog of equations (
26
) and (
27
) for the mixed
H_{S\,\widetilde{S}}
and
r_{AdS}\,\neq\,\infty
:

\displaystyle T_{\widetilde{-}\,S\,\widetilde{S}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,=\,P_{\widetilde{-}}\,H_{S\,\widetilde{S}}\,+\,D_{\widetilde{S}}\,H_{\widetilde{-}\,S}\,+\,D_{S}\,H_{\widetilde{S}\,\widetilde{-}}\,+\,H_{[\,\widetilde{-}|\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{S\,\widetilde{S}\,]\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

(31)

In equation (
31
) we have term
H_{\widetilde{-}\,S}
zero by gauge choice. The vielbein
H_{\widetilde{S}\,\widetilde{-}}
is proportional to
f_{S\,\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}
. We see that this term is zero after evaluating
f_{S\,\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,=\,0
by (
23
). Using the (
26
) and (
27
) for
{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{A}$}}}}\,=\,S
and
{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{B}$}}}}\,=\,-
and keeping
f_{-\,\widetilde{-}\,S}
nonzero, we get
H_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{S}$}}}}\,-}\,=\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,f_{-\,\widetilde{-}\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,H_{S\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}}
and similarly for
H_{\widetilde{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{S}$}}}}}\,\widetilde{-}}
and so the third term in (
31
) is also fixed. The (
31
) is then:

T_{\widetilde{-}\,S\,\widetilde{S}}\,=\,0\,=\,P_{\widetilde{-}}\,H_{S\,\widetilde{S}}\,+D_{S}{\Big(}\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{-}}}\,\,f_{-\,\widetilde{-}\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,H_{\widetilde{S}\,{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}}\,{\Big)}\,+\,H_{\,S\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\widetilde{S}\,\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

(32)

Using equations (
26
), (
27
), (
32
) and the mixed light-cone gauge together with keeping the mixed structure constants and evaluating the explicit actions of the
S
and
\widetilde{S}
derivatives we get the first important result for the structure of the
H_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{S}$}}}}{\widetilde{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{S}$}}}}}}}
vielbein (in the
AdS
case), see table (
1
) in Appendices.
From the table (
1
) we can see that the possibly nonzero
H_{S\,\widetilde{S}}
in (
32
) are those for which
f_{\widetilde{S}\,\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}
are nonzero (after evaluation of mixed structure constants). By simple
\mbox{left}\,\leftrightarrow\,\mbox{right}
swap we get that for
H_{S\,\widetilde{S}}
to be nonzero also
f_{S\,-\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}
has to be nonzero. From the
[\,S,\,P\,]
part of
SO\,(\,10\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,10\,)
extended algebra of (
2
) we can see the only possibility:
H_{\bf{+a}\,\widetilde{\bf{+c}}}\,\neq\,0
. All the other components of
H_{S\,\widetilde{S}}\,=\,0
by (
32
) and table (
1
) after evaluation. This is a first hint that we are on the right track. The
H_{\bf{+a}\,\widetilde{\bf{+c}}}\,\neq\,0
is the only nonzero part (after evaluation of mixed structure constants) of
H_{S\,\widetilde{S}}
piece, it has a scalar trace and we can easily relate it to
H_{{\bf{a}}\,\widetilde{{\bf{b}}}}\,\equiv\,H_{P\,\widetilde{P}}
, where we expect the part of the pre-potential to be (the symmetric part corresponds to the metric).

To see how
H_{P\,\widetilde{P}}
is related to
H_{S\,\widetilde{S}}
consider the third relation from table (
1
) and after evaluation of mixed structure constants:

\displaystyle(\,1\,+\,{\textstyle{1\over 2\,(r_{AdS})^{2}}}\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{{-}}\,P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,)\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,=\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{-}}}\,H_{{\bf{a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}

(33)

We want to reduce
H_{\bf{a}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
further to get
H_{\bf{a}\,\widetilde{b}}
. One can naïvely expect to just hit
H_{{\bf{a}}\,\widetilde{{\bf{+b}}}}
with
P_{\widetilde{-}}
to get rid of the
\widetilde{S}
index (or alternatively hit by
P_{-}
the
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{b}}}
). It works but one has to be more careful since in the
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
space one has the mixed structure constant
f_{{\bf{-}}\,\widetilde{\bf{b}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,\neq\,0
. To see what is this structure constant (after the mixed structure constants evaluation) we remind that in (
23
) we saw that dimension 2 structure constant is given as
R_{P\,\widetilde{P}\,\mathchar 262}\,\equiv\,R_{{\bf a}\,\widetilde{{\bf b}}}{}^{\bf cd}\,\propto\,\,\,T_{\widetilde{{\bf b}}\,\alpha\,}{}^{\widetilde{\beta}}\,R_{\widetilde{\beta}\,\gamma\,}{}^{\bf cd}\,\gamma_{\bf a}{}^{\alpha\,\gamma}
. We have to be careful with the indices in the
R_{{\bf a}\,\widetilde{{\bf b}}}{}^{\bf cd}
. The
\mathchar 262
indices
cd
in (
23
) were indices for
SO\,(\,5\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,4,\,1\,)
local Lorentz group (or its Wick rotated version
SO\,(\,5\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,5\,)
). But the index
\mathcal{N}
in
f_{{\bf{-}}\,\widetilde{\bf{b}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,\neq\,0
includes the indices for the full
SO\,(\,10\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,10\,)
. We have already made the claim that the original local Lorentz group
SO\,(\,5\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,5\,)
is in the
diagonal
subgroup of the
SO\,(\,10\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,10\,)
, i.e. we have the following (at the level of algebras)
so\,(\,10\,)\,\oplus\,so\,(\,10\,)\,\equiv{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,(so\,(\,10\,)\,+\,so\,(\,10\,))\,\oplus\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,(so\,(\,10\,)\,-\,so\,(\,10\,))\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,so(\,10\,)_{\bf{D}}\,\oplus\,so(\,10\,)_{\bf{Off}}
. (The meaning of previous is to do the operations on basis. The
so\,(\,10\,)\,-\,so\,(\,10\,)
means for example to combine e.g. Lorentz generators like:
S\,-\,{\widetilde{S}}\,=\,S_{\bf{Off}}
and similarly for another generators). Now the
so(\,5\,)\,\oplus\,so(\,5\,)\,\hookrightarrow\,so(\,10\,)_{\bf{D}}
.
Let us write the last sequence of algebras more precisely. Using indices, the diagonal subgroup (subalgebra) is
so(\,10\,)_{\bf{D}}\,\equiv\,S^{\bf{D}}{}_{\bf{ab}}\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,(\,S_{\scriptsize{\bf{ab}}}\,+\,S_{\widetilde{\bf{ab}}}\,)\,=\,(\,S^{{\bf{D}}}{}_{\bf{ij}},\,S^{{\bf{D}}}{}_{\bf{kl}},\,S^{{\bf{D}}}{}_{\bf{ik}}\,)
, where
{\bf{i}}\,\in\,\{\,{{10}},\,{{1}},\,{{2}},\,{{3}},\,{{4}}\,\}
and
{\bf{k}}\,\in\,\{\,{{5}},\,{{6}},\,{{7}},\,{{8}},\,{{9}}\,\}
and the
{\bf{a}}\,=\,(\,{\bf{i}},\,{\bf{k}}\,)\,\equiv\,\{\,{{1}},\dots,\,{{10}}\,\}
. The
S^{{\bf{D}}}{}_{\bf{ij}}
and
S^{{\bf{D}}}{}_{\bf{kl}}
are the generators of the
SO\,(\,5\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,5\,)
. Moreover, the previous definitions give precise embedding of those operators.

Defining
S^{{\bf{D}}}{}_{\bf{ij}}
and
S^{{\bf{D}}}{}_{\bf{kl}}
we can see that the structure constant
f_{{\bf{a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{b}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}
is either 0 or given by the appropriate
R_{P\,\widetilde{P}\,\mathchar 262_{\bf{D}}}
. We included a small subindex to the
\mathchar 262
coordinate just to remind us that the
\mathchar 262
coordinate is now for the
SO\,(\,5\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,5\,)
diagonal subgroup of
SO\,(\,10\,)_{\bf{D}}
only.

Finally, taking definitions of
R_{{\bf a}\,\widetilde{{\bf b}}}{}^{\bf cd}
and table (
23
) and our definitions we can see that
f_{{\bf{a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{b}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}
is coming from the mixed commutator
[\,P_{\bf{a}},\,P_{\widetilde{\bf{b}}}\,]\propto\begin{cases}\hfill({\textstyle{1\over r_{AdS}}})^{2}\,S^{\bf{D}}{}_{\bf{ab}}\hfill&\text{ if {\bf{a}} \& {\bf{b}}}\,\in\,\{\,{{10}},\,{{1}},\,{{2}},\,{{3}},\,{{4}}\,\}\\
\hfill-\,({\textstyle{1\over r_{AdS}}})^{2}\,S^{\bf{D}}{}_{\bf{ab}}\hfill&\text{ if {\bf{a}} \& {\bf{b}}}\,\in\,\{\,{{5}},\,{{6}},\,{{7}},\,{{8}},\,{{9}}\,\}\\
\hfill 0&\text{otherwise}\\
\end{cases}

The proportionality constant is
{\bf{c}}_{1}\,=\,-\,2
. With the previous definition and with
P_{-}\,\equiv\,-\,=\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,(\,{{10}}\,-\,{{9}}\,)
and
{\widetilde{\bf{b}}\,\in\,\{\,{{5}},\,{{6}},\,{{7}},\,{{8}},\,{{9}}\,\}}
we will get the
[\,P_{-},P_{\widetilde{{\bf{b}}}}\,]\,\propto\,S^{\bf{D}}{}_{{\bf{9}}\,{\bf{b}}}\,=\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,(\,S_{{\bf{9}}\,{\bf{b}}}\,+\,S_{\widetilde{{\bf{9}}\,{\bf{b}}}}\,)
we can also use
P_{\bf{9}}\,=\,(\,P_{+}\,-\,P_{-}\,)
and so
[\,P_{-},P_{\widetilde{{\bf{b}}}}\,]\,\propto\,(\,S_{{\bf{+}}\,{\bf{b}}}\,+\,S_{\widetilde{\bf{+}\,{\bf{b}}}}\,-\,S_{{\bf{-}}\,{\bf{b}}}\,-\,S_{\widetilde{\bf{-}\,{\bf{b}}}}\,)
. Knowing the last relation we can proceed and hit the result of (
33
) by
P_{\widetilde{-}}
and relate
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{{\bf{+b}}}}
with
H_{{\bf{a}}\,\widetilde{{\bf{b}}}}
for the evaluated version. For non-evaluated version we need to do the same for the non-evaluated version of (
33
), that is the third top equation in table (
1
). For vielbein
H_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,{\bf{a}}}
needed in this procedure we get:

\displaystyle 0\,=\,T_{{P}\,\widetilde{P}\,\widetilde{S}}\,\equiv\,T_{\bf{a}\,\widetilde{-}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{[\,{\bf{a}}}\,H_{\widetilde{\bf{-}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,]}\,+\,H_{[\,{\bf{a}}\,|\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\widetilde{\bf{-}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,]\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

(34)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\,P_{\widetilde{-}}\,H_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,{\bf{a}}}\,-\,P_{{\bf{a}}}\,H_{\widetilde{-}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,-\,H_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{{\bf{a}}\,\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,-\,H_{{\bf{a}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\widetilde{-}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

The term
H_{\widetilde{\bf{-}}\,{\bf{a}}}
vanishes because of mixed light-cone gauge, the term
H_{\widetilde{-}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
is fixed by the torsion
T_{-\,\widetilde{\bf{-}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,=0
and use of mixed light-cone gauge similarly as in equation (
A.1
). By that we get:

\displaystyle H_{\widetilde{\bf{-}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,=\,-\,f_{-\,\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{-}}}\,H_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,\rightsquigarrow\,0

(35)

The last term in (
34
) is just
\eta_{-\,+}\,\eta_{{\bf{a}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}
, the analog term as in (
33
). The extra mixed term (after evaluation) in (
34
) is
H_{\bf{+a}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\widetilde{\bf{b}}\,-\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,\propto\,(\,{\textstyle{1\over r_{AdS}}}\,)^{2}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
. Plugging (
35
) into (
34
) and then the result (that is the fixed vielbein
H_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,\bf{a}}
) into the third top equation in table (
1
) we obtain the non-evaluated relation between
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
:

\displaystyle P_{{-}}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,f_{-\,\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,S_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,(\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,)\,-\,f_{-\,\widetilde{\bf{b}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,

(36)

\displaystyle-\,f_{-\,\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,P_{\bf{a}}\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{-}}}\,H_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,+\,f_{\widetilde{-}\,\bf{a}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,{\textstyle{1\over\,P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,H_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,+\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,H_{{\bf{a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{b}}}

after evaluation of the mixed structure constants in (
36
) we get:

\displaystyle H_{{\bf{a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{b}}}\,=\,{\Big(}\,P_{{-}}\,P_{\widetilde{-}}\,+\,{\textstyle{1\over(r_{AdS})^{2}}}\,{\Big)}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}

(37)

5.3
Torsion constraints and
H_{D\,S}
vielbein

To identify what combination of vielbeins gives the pre-potential, we first repeat the properties we are looking for. We are looking for combination of vielbeins (of the low dimension), that has a scalar contraction and is annihilated by certain combination of
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
(see indices defined above (
13
)). Moreover the combination has to be annihilated by the properly defined
{\mathcal{P}}_{+}
operator in the
R\,\rightarrow\,0
limit (still to be defined).

To start, we have one nontrivial hint. We showed that the vielbein
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
is nonzero and is related to the
H_{{\bf{a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{b}}}
. So we can examine what is the action of the
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
on
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
, i.e. we look at the torsion constraint:

\displaystyle T_{D\,S\,\widetilde{S}}\,\equiv\,T_{\alpha^{\prime}\,{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,=\,0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{[\alpha^{\prime}}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}})}\,+\,H_{[\alpha^{\prime}\,|\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\,{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}})\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

(38)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,+\,S_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,{\bf{+a}}}\,+\,S_{\bf{+a}}\,H_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,+\,H_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\alpha^{\prime}\,{\bf{+a}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,+\,S_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,{\bf{+a}}}\,+\,S_{\bf{+a}}\,H_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,+\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,(\gamma_{\bf{+a}})_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta}\,H_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,\beta}

(39)

In the (
39
) we can see various terms with the
S
derivatives. If we could evaluate mixed structure constants before an action of
S
derivatives, those
S
terms in (
39
) would vanish (because the relevant vielbeins are proportional to vanishing mixed constants as we will see). Now they will nontrivially contribute. We note again that we still have the
f_{\widetilde{S}\,D\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,\equiv\,f_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,\alpha^{\prime}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,=\,0\,=\,f_{S\,\widetilde{S}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,\equiv\,f_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}
. The vielbeins
H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,{\bf{+a}}}
and
H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
are fixed by torsion constraints
T_{\widetilde{P}\,D\,S}\,=\,T_{\widetilde{-}\,\alpha^{\prime}\,{\bf{+a}}}\,=\,0\,=\,T_{P\,D\,\widetilde{S}}\,=\,T_{-\,\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
and some few other torsion constraints, as will be shown. We note that as in (
26
) and (
27
) we almost always have the strategy to use invertibility of
P_{-}\,\mbox{and}\,P_{\widetilde{-}}
together with our mixed left-right light cone gauge to eliminate/fix vielbeins. Sometimes its not enough and we need to explore some further torsion constraints. Let’s look at the already mentioned set of torsion constraints:

\displaystyle T_{\widetilde{P}\,D\,S}\,=\,T_{\widetilde{-}\,\alpha^{\prime}\,{\bf{+a}}}\,=\,0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{[\widetilde{-}}\,H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,{\bf{+a}}]}\,+\,H_{[\widetilde{-}\,|\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\alpha^{\prime}\,{\bf{+a}}]\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

(40)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{\widetilde{-}}\,H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,{\bf{+a}}}\,+\,H_{\bf{+a}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\widetilde{-}\,\alpha^{\prime}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

(41)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,{\bf{+a}}}\,=\,f_{\widetilde{-}\,\alpha^{\prime}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,\rightsquigarrow\,0

(42)

in (
40
) we used just the mixed light cone gauge and
f_{S\,\widetilde{P}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,=\,0
(in flat case and also in
AdS
). To evaluate the last term in (
41
) that is present only in
AdS
case we have to take the
AdS
curvature
T_{\widetilde{P}\,D\,\widetilde{\mathchar 266}}\,\equiv\,T_{\widetilde{\bf{a}}\,\alpha}{}^{\widetilde{\beta}}\,\equiv\,f_{\widetilde{{\bf{a}}}\,\alpha}{}^{\widetilde{\beta}}\,=\,{\textstyle{1\over r_{AdS}}}\,(\gamma_{\bf{a}})_{\alpha\,\nu}\,\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5}{}^{\nu\,\beta}
as discussed above (
23
). For our specific indices we have
{\textstyle{1\over r_{AdS}}}\,(\gamma_{\bf{-}})_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\nu^{\prime}}\,\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5}{}^{\nu^{\prime}\,\beta}
but the
(\gamma_{\bf{-}})_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\nu^{\prime}}\,=\,0
as we can see in the construction of the light cone basis for the gamma matrices in (
13
). The vielbein
H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,{\bf{-a}}}
can be fixed in almost the same set of equations as
H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,{\bf{+b}}}
. Fixing the vielbein
H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
(and similarly
H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{-b}}}
) is also similar but a bit more profound. For that we first examine torsion analogous to (
38
) but for
H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
vielbein:

\displaystyle T_{P\,D\,\widetilde{S}}\,\equiv\,T_{{\bf{-}}\,\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,=\,0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{[-}\,H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}]}\,+\,H_{[-\,|\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}]\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

(43)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{-}\,H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,+\,S_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,H_{-\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,+\,H_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{-\,\alpha^{\prime}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,=\,-\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{-}}}\,S_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,H_{-\,\alpha^{\prime}}

(44)

The (
43
) structure constant
f_{-\,\alpha^{\prime}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,\propto\,(\gamma_{-})_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\nu^{\prime}}
but as before that particular piece of gamma matrix is zero (remember the non-mixed structure constants are not breaking the
SO\,(\,10\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,10\,)
so we can evaluate them without any concern). The other term in (
43
) is
H_{-\,\alpha^{\prime}}
. That is fixed by the dim
{\textstyle{1\over 2}}
torsion constraint
T_{-\,\widetilde{-}\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,=\,0
:

\displaystyle T_{P\,\widetilde{P}\,D}\,=\,T_{-\,\widetilde{-}\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,=\,0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{[-}\,H_{\widetilde{-}\,\alpha^{\prime}\,)}\,+\,H_{[-\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\widetilde{-}\,\alpha^{\prime}\,)\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

(45)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{\widetilde{-}}\,H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,-}\,+\,H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{-\,\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

(46)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,-}\,=\,-\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,f_{-\,\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,\rightsquigarrow\,0

(47)

We used in (
45
) the mixed light cone gauge, also the fact that
f_{-\,\alpha^{\prime}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\propto\,(\gamma_{-})_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta^{\prime}}\,=\,0
. We note that the (
45
) evaluates to zero because the mixed structure constant
f_{-\,\widetilde{-}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,=\,0
. Moreover by the light-cone gauge the (
45
) term
H_{-\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\widetilde{-}\,\alpha^{\prime}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,=\,0
even in the non-evaluated regime. The reason is that the structure constant
f_{\widetilde{-}\,\alpha^{\prime}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}
is zero after evaluation and the action of whatever
S
on this structure constant produces either zero or the right
D
index
\equiv\,\widetilde{D}
(after the summation with the vielbein) i.e. the vielbein
H_{-\,\widetilde{D}}
that is again zero by the light-cone gauge. Combining (
44
) and (
47
) we can get a fixed version of the
H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
. By the similar equations as above we can fix
H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{-b}}}
. That result and more detailed analysis is shown in the Appendix, see table (
2
).

In the Appendix, we also derived the equations (
A.13
) and (
A.35
). Those are the actions of the
S
and
\widetilde{S}
derivatives that we need in the equation (
39
). Putting the results from (
A.13
) and (
A.35
) into (
39
) we get fixing of the
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,H_{\bf{+a}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
, we note that this is an important result:

\displaystyle D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\,{\textstyle{1\over g}}\,{\textstyle{1\over(r_{AdS})\,P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,(\gamma_{{\bf{b}}})_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\sigma}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})^{\sigma\,\beta}\,H_{\widetilde{\beta}\,{\bf{+a}}}\,+\,{\textstyle{1\over 2\,g}}\,(\,1\,-\,{\textstyle{1\over f}}\,{\textstyle{1\over(r_{AdS})^{2}\,P_{\widetilde{-}}\,P_{-}}}\,)(\gamma_{\bf{+a}})_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta}\,H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,

(48)

\displaystyle+\,{\textstyle{1\over f\,g}}\,{\textstyle{1\over 2\,(r_{AdS})^{3}\,P_{-}\,(P_{\widetilde{-}})^{2}}}\,\,(\gamma_{\bf{a}})_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\nu}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})^{\nu\,\beta}\,H_{\widetilde{\beta}\,{\bf{+b}}}

where
f
and
g
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=

\displaystyle(\,1\,-\,{\textstyle{1\over 2\,(r_{AdS})^{2}\,P_{\widetilde{-}}\,P_{-}}}\,)

(49)

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=

\displaystyle(\,1\,-\,{\textstyle{1\over f}}\,{\textstyle{1\over 2\,(r_{AdS})^{2}\,P_{\widetilde{-}}\,P_{-}\,}}\,)

Changing
\mbox{left}\,\leftrightarrow\,\mbox{right}
in (
48
) we get the equation for
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
. There is one simplification we can make in equations (
48
). Because only half of the block diagonal
\gamma_{+}
matrix is nonzero and is proportional to the
\delta
for the nonzero part. The
(\gamma_{\bf{+a}})_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta}\,=\,\delta_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\nu^{\prime}}\,(\gamma_{\bf{a}})^{\nu^{\prime}\,\beta}\,\equiv\,(\gamma_{\bf{a}})_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta}
.

The observation from (
48
) and its
\mbox{left}\,\leftrightarrow\,\mbox{right}
swap is that the action of the
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
on
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
is producing two new vielbeins
H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}
and
H_{\widetilde{\beta}\,{\bf{+a}}}
. This hints that we need some another vielbein, such that the action of
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
on it will effectively subtract the fields
H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}
and
H_{\widetilde{\beta}\,{\bf{+a}}}
. We found such a vielbein, but before giving it we will look at the flat case superspace first to give a motivation. After that we will generalise it to the
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
background.

6
Flat space solution

6.1
Flat space diagram

To see what could be possibly a missing vielbein that will subtract vielbeins in (
48
) (and its
\mbox{left}\,\leftrightarrow\,\mbox{right}
change) we first solve the same problem in flat space background. That is the extended superspace with
r_{AdS}\,\rightarrow\infty
. Note that in flat superspace (
r_{AdS}\,\rightarrow\,\infty
) the relation (
48
) simplifies significantly, because there are no
r_{AdS}
dependent parts. The surviving part after
r_{AdS}\,\rightarrow\,\infty
is just the second term on the right hand side of (
48
) with
g\,=\,1
, i.e.
{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,(\gamma_{\bf{+a}})_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta}\,H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
.

Let us therefore further examine an action of
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
on
H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}
and
H_{\widetilde{\beta}\,{\bf{+a}}}
respectively:

\displaystyle T_{D\,D\,\widetilde{S}}\,\equiv\,T_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}\,=\,0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{[\alpha^{\prime}}\,H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+a}})}\,+\,H_{[\alpha^{\prime}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+a}}\,)\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

(50)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}\,+\,S_{\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}\,H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta}\,-\,D_{\beta}\,H_{\widetilde{\bf{+a}}\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,+\,H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+a}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,

\displaystyle+\,H_{\widetilde{\bf{+a}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,-\,H_{\beta\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\widetilde{\bf{+a}}\,\alpha^{\prime}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

The mixed terms in the
f
part of (
50
) are zero (note they are zero also in the
AdS
background). The structure constant
f_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,=\,2\,(\gamma_{\bf{a}})_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta}\,\delta^{\bf{a}}{}_{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}
(the same is in the
AdS
background). The vielbein
H_{\widetilde{\bf{+a}}\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,\rightsquigarrow\,0
by the table (
2
). Then the equation (
50
) can be rewritten as:

\displaystyle 0\,=\,D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}\,+\,S_{\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}\,H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta}\,+\,2\,(\gamma^{\bf{c}})_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta}\,H_{\widetilde{\bf{+a}}\,{\bf{c}}}

(51)

To evaluate the only
S
derivative term in (
51
) i.e.
S_{\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}\,H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta}
we would need to work a bit, in the
AdS
superspace. The whole
AdS
analysis of the actions of
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
on
H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}
and
H_{\widetilde{\beta}\,{\bf{+a}}}
is done in the Appendix, see equations (
A.39
) till (
A.63
). In this section we would need only
r_{AdS}\,\rightarrow\,\infty
limit of that analysis.

In the Appendix we derived the equations (
A.57
) and (
A.63
). Those equations are telling us that in the
AdS
case (and so also in the flat case) the actions of
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
and
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
result in a combination of
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
and
H_{\alpha\,\widetilde{\beta}}
. This is actually a hint that we should add the trace of
H_{\alpha\,\widetilde{\beta}}
to the trace of
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
in order to subtract an action of a linear combination of
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
(future
D^{v}
derivative) on trace
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
. In the rest of this paragraph and next chapter we will look at how the pre-potential is built up in a flat space limit, i.e. we consider equations (
A.57
) and (
A.58
) and (
A.63
) and (
A.64
) in the limit
r_{AdS}\,\rightarrow\,\infty
. We find equations that are fixing pre-potential and vanishing
D_{w}
derivative.

Thus we repeat the flat space limits of the
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
and
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
actions on
H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}
and
H_{\widetilde{\beta}\,{\bf{+a}}}
respectively, i.e. the equations (
A.63
) and (
A.64
) in
r_{AdS}\,\rightarrow\,\infty
limit:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=
