\displaystyle D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}\,+\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,(\gamma_{\bf{a}})_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\nu}H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\nu}}

(52)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{\alpha^{\prime}}H_{\widetilde{\beta}\,{\bf{+a}}}\,+\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,(\gamma_{\bf{a}})_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\nu}H_{\nu\,\widetilde{\beta}}

(53)

Similarly, we can also look at the equations (
48
) and (
A.57
) and (
A.58
) (in the flat space limit) and together with (
52
) and (
53
) we can observe the following interesting flat space diagram:

\textstyle{H_{{\bf{+a}}\,{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{D_{\alpha^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{D_{\alpha^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{H_{{\bf{c}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}}
\textstyle{H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{\alpha^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\beta}}}
\textstyle{H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}}

(54)

The scheme (
54
) is nice and actually tells us what we should do next. Recall that the nodes
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
and
H_{{\bf{c}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
and
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}
could be identified by the use of invertible operators
P_{-}
and
P_{\widetilde{-}}
see (
33
). Our original aim was find a field that has a scalar trace and could possibly subtract actions of
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
on
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
. We will see that the missing field is exactly
H_{\beta\widetilde{\beta}}
(in the flat space, the only nonzero part of
H_{D\,\widetilde{D}}
). The diagram (
54
) suggests what to do. We calculate the remaining arrows and fill the square.

To fill the remaining arrows we need to calculate the action of
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
on
H_{\alpha\,\widetilde{\beta}}
together with some another arrows that will be discussed later. We consider the dimension
{\textstyle{1\over 2}}
torsion constraint
T_{D\,D\,\widetilde{D}}\,\equiv\,T_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta\,\widetilde{\sigma}}\,=\,0
. We note again that for now on we are working in the flat space. Later we will generalise the procedure for the
AdS
space:

\displaystyle T_{D\,D\,\widetilde{D}}\,\equiv\,T_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta\,\widetilde{\sigma}}\,=\,0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{(\alpha^{\prime}}H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\sigma})}\,+\,H_{(\alpha^{\prime}\,|\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\beta\,\widetilde{\sigma}\,)\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

(55)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{\alpha^{\prime}}H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\sigma}}\,+\,2\,(\gamma^{\bf{a}})_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta}\,H_{\widetilde{\sigma}\,{\bf{a}}}

(56)

where we used that
H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta}\,=\,H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\sigma}}\,=\,0
in flat space (see (
A.40
) and (
A.45
) and do flat space limit). We also have a
\mbox{left}\,\leftrightarrow\,\mbox{right}
swap of (
56
). The vielbein
H_{\widetilde{\sigma}\,{\bf{c}}}
in (
56
) is related to
H_{{\bf{+c}}\,\widetilde{\sigma}}
. For that consider torsion constraint
T_{P\,S\,\widetilde{D}}\,\equiv\,T_{-\,{\bf{+c}}\,\widetilde{\sigma}}\,=\,0
:

\displaystyle T_{P\,S\,\widetilde{D}}\,\equiv\,T_{-\,{\bf{+c}}\,\widetilde{\sigma}}\,=\,0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{[-}H_{{\bf{+c}}\,\widetilde{\sigma})}\,+\,H_{[-\,|\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{{\bf{+c}}\,\widetilde{\sigma}\,)\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

(57)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{-}H_{{\bf{+c}}\,\widetilde{\sigma}}\,+\,\eta_{+-}\,H_{\widetilde{\sigma}\,{\bf{c}}}

(58)

where we used left-right light-cone gauge, together with
H_{-\,{\bf{+c}}}\,=\,0
that is shown in the Appendix and holds even in
AdS
, see (
A.20
).

To fill the diagram (
54
) we need to calculate two more torsion constraints that are providing the actions of
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
on
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}
and on
H_{\sigma\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}
. We first consider
T_{D\,S\,\widetilde{P}}\,\equiv\,T_{\alpha^{\prime}\,{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}\,=\,0
:

\displaystyle T_{D\,S\,\widetilde{P}}\,\equiv\,T_{\alpha^{\prime}\,{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}\,=\,0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{[\alpha^{\prime}}H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}})}\,+\,H_{[\alpha^{\prime}\,|\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}})\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

(59)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{\alpha^{\prime}}H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}\,+\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,(\gamma_{\bf{+a}})_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\sigma}\,H_{\widetilde{\bf{c}}\,\sigma}

(60)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{\alpha^{\prime}}H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}\,+\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,(\gamma_{\bf{a}})_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\sigma}\,H_{\widetilde{\bf{c}}\,\sigma}

(61)

where we used that
H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,{\bf{+a}}}\,=\,0
(holds even in the
AdS
, see table (
2
)). We also used that
H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}\,=\,0
(that is enough in a flat space to have
S_{\bf{+a}}\,H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}\,=\,0
). To see that
H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}\,=\,0
we use the torsion
T_{\widetilde{P}\,\widetilde{S}\,D}\,\equiv\,T_{\widetilde{-}\,\widetilde{\bf{+c}}\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,=\,0
:

\displaystyle T_{\widetilde{P}\,\widetilde{S}\,D}\,\equiv\,T_{\widetilde{-}\,\widetilde{\bf{+c}}\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,=\,0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{[\widetilde{-}}H_{\widetilde{\bf{+c}}\,\alpha^{\prime})}\,+\,H_{[\widetilde{-}\,|\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\widetilde{\bf{+c}}\,\alpha^{\prime}\,)\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

(62)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{\widetilde{-}}H_{\widetilde{\bf{+c}}\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,+\,\eta_{+-}\,H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}

(63)

and previously we saw that
H_{\widetilde{\bf{+c}}\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,=\,0
(even in the
AdS
case, see table (
2
)). From (
63
) in the flat case follows that
H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}\,=\,0
. Examining the (
63
) in the
AdS
case one also finds that
H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}\,=\,0
(after evaluation). The (
61
) however could have some additional term in the
AdS
case. The structure constant
f_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,\neq\,0
and so the term proportional to that structure constant in the
AdS
case is
{\textstyle{1\over r_{AdS}}}\,(\gamma_{\bf{c}})_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\sigma}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})^{\sigma\,\nu}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\nu}}
. That term is nonzero in the
AdS
case. Moreover, in the (
59
) one finds one more
AdS
term, coming from evaluated action
S_{\bf{+a}}\,H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}
. Those terms are not of a big concern right now (doing the flat space first), we will see them later in the section where we generalise to
AdS
case.

Last torsion constraint to examine in order to fill the (
54
) is the one that determines the action of
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
on
H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}
. Consider therefore the dimension
{\textstyle{1\over 2}}
torsion
T_{D\,D\,\widetilde{P}}\,\equiv\,T_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}\,=\,0
:

\displaystyle T_{D\,D\,\widetilde{P}}\,\equiv\,T_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}\,=\,0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{[\alpha^{\prime}}H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{c}})}\,+\,H_{[\alpha^{\prime}\,|\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}\,)\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

(64)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}\,+\,2\,(\gamma^{\bf{a}})_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta}\,H_{\widetilde{\bf{c}}\,{\bf{a}}}

(65)

where we used the
H_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta}\,=\,0
(holds also in
AdS
after the evaluation) and
H_{\widetilde{\bf{c}}\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,=\,0
(also holds in
AdS
after the evaluation). In the
AdS
case in the equation (
65
) we have two additional terms. They come from
f_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,\neq\,0
and also
f_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,\neq\,0
. Those terms will be further analysed in future sections, let just write their structure as
{\textstyle{1\over r_{AdS}}}\,(\gamma_{\bf{c}})_{\beta\,\nu^{\prime}}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})^{\nu^{\prime}\,\sigma^{\prime}}\,H_{\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}\,\alpha^{\prime}}
and
{\textstyle{1\over r_{AdS}}}\,(\gamma_{\bf{c}})_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\nu}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})^{\nu\,\sigma}\,H_{\widetilde{\sigma}\,\beta}
. The vielbein
H_{\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,=\,0
(in flat case and also in
AdS
after the evaluation) as can be calculated from torsion constraints
T_{-\,\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,=\,0
and
T_{\widetilde{-}\,-\,\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}}\,=\,0
and the use of the double light-cone gauge. The term
H_{\widetilde{\sigma}\,\beta}
is nonzero (
H_{\widetilde{\sigma}\,\beta}
vielbein is a part of a pre-potential).

We can add results of (flat space) equations (
61
) and (
65
) together with (
56
) and their
\mbox{left}\,\leftrightarrow\,\mbox{right}
swaps to the diagram (
54
) and find the following square diagram:

\textstyle{H_{{\bf{+a}}\,{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{D_{\alpha^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{D_{\alpha^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{H_{{\bf{c}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{\alpha^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{\alpha^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{H_{{\bf{c}}\,\widetilde{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{\alpha^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{\alpha^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{H_{\bf{c}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}}

(66)

As we saw before the (
66
) nodes
\{\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}},\,H_{{\bf{c}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}},\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}},\,H_{{\bf{c}}\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}\,\}
should be identified (as one node). We proved that using various torsion constraints, mixed light-cone gauge and invertibility of
P_{-}
and
P_{\widetilde{-}}
. The same way the nodes
\{\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\beta}},\,H_{{\bf{c}}\,\widetilde{\beta}}\,\}
and independently nodes
\{\,H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}},\,H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{c}}}\,\}
should be identified (as two independent nodes). The vielbein
H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\beta}}
is then just a single node. After the described identifications the diagram (
66
) could be rewritten in the simpler and more informative form.

\textstyle{H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{\alpha^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{D_{{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{D_{\alpha^{\prime}}}

(67)

Note, the dashed arrows stand for action of
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
and solid arrows stand for action of
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
. From the nice flat space diagram (
67
) it is obvious that in order to have a vanishing derivative we have to combine
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
with
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
and that combination should act on the combination of traces of
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
with
H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\beta}}
.

6.2
The
{\mathbb{H}}
matrix

The diagram (
67
) could be rewritten in the matrix form. The observation is that each action of the derivatives in the (
67
) is given by some matrix. The derivatives are mixing fields just as in (
67
). Let us introduce the
2\,\otimes\,2
block matrix
\mathbb{H}
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{H}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\begin{pmatrix}H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}&H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\beta}}\\
H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}&H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\beta}}\end{pmatrix}

(68)

The action of
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
is then given as the left action of some constant (up to
P_{-}
operator) block off diagonal matrix
\mathcal{\mathchar 256_{\alpha^{\prime}}}
:

\displaystyle D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\begin{pmatrix}H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}&H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\beta}}\\
H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}&H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\beta}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}0&{\textstyle{1\over 2}}(\gamma_{{\bf{a}}})_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\sigma}\\
2\,P_{-}\,(\gamma^{{\bf{c}}})_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta}&0\end{pmatrix}\,\begin{pmatrix}H_{{\bf{+c}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}&H_{{\bf{+c}}\,\widetilde{\beta}}\\
H_{\sigma\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}&H_{\sigma\,\widetilde{\beta}}\end{pmatrix}

(69)

\displaystyle D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathchar 256_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,{\mathbb{H}}

(70)

The action of
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
on
\mathbb{H}
is given as a right action of similar matrix
\mathchar 256_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
:

\displaystyle D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}\begin{pmatrix}H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}&H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\beta}}\\
H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}&H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\beta}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+c}}}&H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\sigma}}\\
H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+c}}}&H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\sigma}}\end{pmatrix}\,\begin{pmatrix}0&2\,P_{\widetilde{-}}\,(\gamma^{{\bf{c}}})_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta}\\
{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,(\gamma_{{\bf{b}}})_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\sigma}&0\end{pmatrix}

(71)

\displaystyle D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}\,{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{H}}\,\mathchar 256_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}

(72)

Now we will proceed to the main step. We arbitrarily linearly combine
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
, i.e. we multiply the
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
with some unknown nonsingular matrix
\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}
:

{\mathcal{D}}_{v}\,\equiv\,{\mathcal{D}}^{v}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,(\,D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,-\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}\,D_{\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}}\,)

(73)

Moreover we impose that in the matrix version of
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
action the matrix
{\mathcal{M}}
acts as follows:

{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}\,\mathchar 256_{\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}}\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,{\mathbb{A}}\,\mathchar 256_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}\,{\mathbb{B}}

(74)

for some nonsingular matrices
{\mathbb{A}}
and
{\mathbb{B}}
. Combining (
69
) and (
71
) together with (
73
) and (
74
) we get:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}^{v}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,{\mathbb{H}}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathchar 256_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,{\mathbb{H}}\,-\,{\mathbb{H}}\,{\mathbb{A}}\,\mathchar 256_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}\,{\mathbb{B}}\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,/\,{\mathbb{B}}^{-1}\,

(75)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}^{v}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,{\mathbb{H}}\,{\mathbb{B}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathchar 256_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,{\mathbb{H}}\,{\mathbb{B}}^{-1}\,-\,{\mathbb{H}}\,{\mathbb{A}}\,\mathchar 256_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,/\,{\mbox{Str}}\,

(76)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}^{v}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,\mbox{Str}\,(\,{\mathbb{H}}\,{\mathbb{B}}^{-1}\,)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{Str}\,{\Big(}(\,{\mathbb{B}}^{-1}\,\mathchar 256_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,-\,{\mathbb{A}}\,\mathchar 256_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}\,)\,{\mathbb{H}}\,{\Big)}

(77)

by the
Str
we mean the super-trace. We put to zero the
\mbox{Str}\,{\Big(}\,(\,{\mathbb{B}}^{-1}\,\mathchar 256_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,-\,{\mathbb{A}}\,\mathchar 256_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}\,)\,{\mathbb{H}}\,{\Big)}\,=\,0
by finding the suitable matrices
{\mathbb{B}}
and
\mathbb{A}
and the matrix
{\mathcal{M}}
. By that we get the equation:

{\mathcal{D}}^{v}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,\mbox{Str}\,(\,{\mathbb{H}}\,{\mathbb{B}}^{-1}\,)\,=\,0

(78)

thus the equation (
78
) defines the
\mbox{Str}\,(\,{\mathbb{H}}\,{\mathbb{B}}^{-1}\,)
as the scalar field on which particular combination of
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
now called
{\mathcal{D}}^{v}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
vanishes. So, we found a pre-potential
V\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,\mbox{Str}\,(\,{\mathbb{H}}\,{\mathbb{B}}^{-1}\,)
. We note that even though the equation (
77
) might seem easy to solve just by putting
{\mathbb{B}^{-1}}\,=\,{\mathbb{A}}
. It is not that simple since
\mathchar 256_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,\neq\,\mathchar 256_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
. Therefore some more involved solution has to be found.

6.3
Solution via the gamma matrix identity

We solve the equation (
77
) using the following identity:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\sigma^{\prime}}\,{\mathcal{B}}_{\bf{a}}{}^{\bf{c}}\,{\mathcal{C}}_{\beta}{}^{\nu}\,(\gamma_{\bf{c}})_{\sigma^{\prime}\,\nu}\,=\,(\gamma_{\bf{a}})_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta}

(79)

has two
SO\,(\,4\,)
invariant solutions:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I.}}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}}\,=\,\delta_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}\,\,\,\,||\,\,\,\,{\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}}\,=\,\delta_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\,\,\,\,||\,\,\,\,{\mathcal{B}}_{\bf{a}}{}^{\bf{b}}\,=\,\delta_{\bf{a}}{}^{\bf{b}}

(80)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{II.}}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}}\,=\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}\,\,\,\,||\,\,\,\,{\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}}\,=\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\,\,\,\,||\,\,\,\,{\mathcal{B}}_{\bf{a}}{}^{\bf{b}}\,=\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\bf{a}}{}^{\bf{b}}

(81)

The solution (
80
) is trivial, the solution (
81
) is based on property of the
\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5}
matrix:
[\,\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5},\,\gamma_{\bf{a}}\,]\,=\,0\,\,\,\mbox{for}\,\,\,{\bf{a}}\,\in\,\{\,{{10}},\,{{1}},\,{{2}},\,{{3}},\,{{4}}\,\}
and
\{\,\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5},\,\gamma_{\bf{a}}\,\}\,=\,0\,\,\,\mbox{for}\,\,\,{\bf{a}}\,\in\,\{\,{{5}},\,{{6}},\,{{7}},\,{{8}},\,{{9}}\,\}
. The previous follows directly from the definition of
\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5}
, see (
12
). The new matrix
(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\bf{a}}{}^{\bf{b}}
in (
81
) is defined by the (
79
) to fix the signs. Note that the indices
{\bf{a}}
in (
79
) have a range:
{\bf{a}}\,\in\,\{\,{{1}},\,\dots\,,{{8}}\}
.

Next, we look explicitly at the equation:

\mbox{Str}\,{\Big(}(\,{\mathbb{B}}^{-1}\,\mathchar 256_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,-\,{\mathbb{A}}\,\mathchar 256_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}\,)\,{\mathbb{H}}\,{\Big)}\,\equiv\,{\mbox{Str}}\,{\mathbb{X}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,=\,0

(82)

let’s rename the members of the matrix
{\mathbb{H}}
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{H}}\,\equiv\begin{pmatrix}H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}&H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\beta}}\\
H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}&H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\beta}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}H_{S\,\widetilde{S}}&H_{S\,\widetilde{D}}\\
H_{D\,\widetilde{S}}&H_{D\,\widetilde{D}}\end{pmatrix}

(83)

Let us define the matrices
{\mathbb{A}}
and
{\mathbb{B}^{-1}}
to be block diagonal matrices. This is a consistent choice with the fact that we want to have a pre-potential build out of
H_{S\,\widetilde{S}}
and
H_{D\,\widetilde{D}}
. The pre-potential is in (
78
) given as
\mbox{Str}\,(\,{\mathbb{H}}\,{\mathbb{B}}^{-1}\,)
. We do not want to mix in some off diagonal
{\mathbb{H}}
fields by the action of
{\mathbb{B}^{-1}}
. Thus
{\mathbb{A}}
and
{\mathbb{B}^{-1}}
are:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{A}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}A_{S\,\widetilde{S}}&0\\
0&A_{D\,\widetilde{D}}\end{pmatrix}\,\,\,\,\,\,\,||\,\,\,\,\,\,\,{\mathbb{B}}^{-1}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}B^{-1}{}_{S\,\widetilde{S}}&0\\
0&B^{-1}{}_{D\,\widetilde{D}}\end{pmatrix}

(84)

With definitions (
84
) we get the equation (
82
) into the following matrix equation:

\displaystyle{\mbox{Str}}\,{\Big(}\,\begin{pmatrix}{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,B^{-1}{}_{S\,\widetilde{S}}\,\gamma\,H_{D\,\widetilde{S}}&\dots\\
\dots&2\,P_{-}\,B^{-1}{}_{D\,\widetilde{D}}\,\gamma\,H_{S\,\widetilde{D}}\end{pmatrix}\,-\,\begin{pmatrix}2\,P_{\widetilde{-}}\,A{}_{S\,\widetilde{S}}\,\gamma\,H_{D\,\widetilde{S}}&\dots\\
\dots&\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,A{}_{D\,\widetilde{D}}\,\gamma\,H_{S\,\widetilde{D}}\end{pmatrix}\,{\Big)}=\,0

(85)

Then from (
85
) we get two equations (since fields
H_{D\,\widetilde{S}}
and
H_{S\,\widetilde{D}}
are independent):

\displaystyle{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,B^{-1}{}_{S\,\widetilde{S}}\,-\,2\,P_{\widetilde{-}}\,A_{S\,\widetilde{S}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,\Rightarrow\,A{}_{S\,\widetilde{S}}\,=\,{\textstyle{1\over 4\,P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,B^{-1}{}_{S\,\widetilde{S}}

(86)

\displaystyle P_{-}\,B^{-1}{}_{D\,\widetilde{D}}\,-\,A_{D\,\widetilde{D}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,\Rightarrow\,A{}_{D\,\widetilde{D}}\,=\,4\,P_{-}\,B^{-1}{}_{D\,\widetilde{D}}

(87)

Now we are prepared to examine the equation (
74
) using the
{\mathbb{A}}
and
{\mathbb{B}}
constructed above. Then the matrix equation (
74
) can be (schematically) written:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}\begin{pmatrix}0&2\,P_{\widetilde{-}}\,\gamma\\
{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,\gamma&0\end{pmatrix}\,=\,\begin{pmatrix}{\textstyle{1\over\,4\,P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,B^{-1}{}_{S\,\widetilde{S}}&0\\
0&4\,P_{-}\,B^{-1}{}_{D\,\widetilde{D}}\end{pmatrix}\,\begin{pmatrix}0&2\,P_{\widetilde{-}}\,\gamma\\
{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,\gamma&0\end{pmatrix}\,\begin{pmatrix}B_{S\,\widetilde{S}}&0\\
0&B_{D\,\widetilde{D}}\end{pmatrix}

(88)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}\begin{pmatrix}0&2\,P_{\widetilde{-}}\,\gamma\\
{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,\gamma&0\end{pmatrix}\,=\,\begin{pmatrix}0&{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,B^{-1}{}_{S\,\widetilde{S}}\,\gamma\,B_{D\,\widetilde{D}}\\
2\,P_{-}\,B^{-1}{}_{D\,\widetilde{D}}\,\gamma\,B_{S\,\widetilde{S}}&0\end{pmatrix}

(89)

We now do the following re-scalings
{\mathcal{M}}\,\rightarrow\,{\textstyle{1\over\lambda}}\,{\mathcal{M}}
and
B_{S\,\widetilde{S}}\,\rightarrow\,\mathchar 257\,B_{S\,\widetilde{S}}
and
B_{D\,\widetilde{D}}\,\rightarrow\,\rho\,B_{D\,\widetilde{D}}
. Rescaled
{\mathcal{M}}
and
B_{S\,\widetilde{S}}
and
B_{D\,\widetilde{D}}
belong to one of the two solutions of identity (
79
). Then we get the version of (
89
):

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}\begin{pmatrix}0&2\,P_{\widetilde{-}}\,\gamma\\
{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,\gamma&0\end{pmatrix}\,=\,\begin{pmatrix}0&{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,{\textstyle{\lambda\,\rho\over\mathchar 257}}\,B^{-1}{}_{S\,\widetilde{S}}\,\gamma\,B_{D\,\widetilde{D}}\\
2\,{\textstyle{\lambda\,\mathchar 257\over\rho}}\,P_{-}\,B^{-1}{}_{D\,\widetilde{D}}\,\gamma\,B_{S\,\widetilde{S}}&0\end{pmatrix}

(90)

Now we want the
\lambda
and
\rho
and
\mathchar 257
to satisfy:

\displaystyle{\textstyle{\lambda\,\rho\over\mathchar 257}}\,=\,4\,P_{\widetilde{-}}\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\mbox{and}\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,{\textstyle{\lambda\,\mathchar 257\over\rho}}\,=\,{\textstyle{1\over 4\,P_{-}}}\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\Rightarrow\,\,\,\lambda\,=\,\pm\,\sqrt{{\textstyle{P_{\widetilde{-}}\over P_{-}}}}\,\,\,\,\,\mbox{and}\,\,\,\,\,\,{\textstyle{\rho\over\mathchar 257}}\,=\,\pm\,4\,\sqrt{P_{\widetilde{-}}\,P_{-}}

(91)

The (
90
) is just a matrix equation:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}\,\begin{pmatrix}0&P_{\widetilde{-}}\,\gamma\\
\gamma&0\end{pmatrix}\,=\,\begin{pmatrix}0&A^{-1}{}_{S\,\widetilde{S}}\,P_{\widetilde{-}}\,\gamma\,A_{D\,\widetilde{D}}\\
A^{-1}{}_{D\,\widetilde{D}}\,\gamma\,A_{S\,\widetilde{S}}&0\end{pmatrix}

(92)

that can be solved by (
79
).
Even though we saw the appearance of the nasty square roots in the (
91
) and so in the definition of
{\mathcal{D}}^{v}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and in the pre-potential via super-trace of
{\mathbb{H\,B}}^{-1}
. We will see in the
AdS
case solution that there is a way how to get rid of it.

7
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
solution

7.1
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
diagram

In the previous sub-sections we saw how to find the pre-potential in the flat case. We are really interested in the
AdS
case. Along the way we analysed the flat case in the previous sub-sections we mentioned also changes one has to make in the
AdS
case. We repeat them here again since they are scattered over the previous flat case sub-sections and in the Appendix.
First change has already been worked out in the relation between
H_{{\bf{+a}\,{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}}}
and
H_{{\bf{a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{b}}}
in (
37
). We also note that there are
AdS
contributions in equations (
48
) also in (
A.57
) and (
A.58
). The nontrivial contributions also appeared in equations (
A.63
) and (
A.64
).

We could visualise the relations (
48
) and (
A.57
) and (
A.58
) and (
A.63
) and (
A.64
) by the similar diagram as used in flat case, see (
54
). The structure is very similar just with more arrows between nodes. Since the
AdS
diagram is messier we will not provide it. The idea is however the same as in the flat case. In order to determine the vanishing
D^{v}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
derivative and the pre-potential we need to combine
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
for
D^{v}
derivative and
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
together with
H_{\alpha\,\widetilde{\beta}}
for pre-potential.

The only missing derivative in the set of
AdS
equations: (
48
) and (
A.57
) and (
A.58
) and (
A.63
) and (
A.64
), is an action of
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
on
H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\sigma}}
. This action can be calculated from
T_{D\,D\,\widetilde{D}}\,\equiv\,T_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta\,\widetilde{\sigma}}\,=\,0
torsion constraint. The
AdS
contribution in that constraint comes from
f_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\sigma}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}
structure constant. We have already analysed this structure constant, see equations (
A.46
) and (
A.47
) till (
A.50
). We can thus directly write the constraint with an extra
AdS
term:

\displaystyle T_{D\,D\,\widetilde{D}}\,\equiv\,T_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta\,\widetilde{\sigma}}\,=\,0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{[\alpha^{\prime}}H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\sigma})}\,+\,H_{[\alpha^{\prime}\,|\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\beta\,\widetilde{\sigma}\,)\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

(93)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\sigma}}\,+\,2\,(\gamma^{\bf{a}})_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta}\,H_{\widetilde{\sigma}\,{\bf{a}}}\,+{\textstyle{1\over r_{AdS}}}\,(\gamma^{\bf{[c}})_{\sigma\,\rho}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})^{\rho\,\nu}\,(\gamma^{\bf{d}]})_{\nu\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,H_{\beta\,{\bf{cd}}}

where we again note that the
\mathchar 262
indices in the last expression of the (
93
) second line are from the
SO(\,5\,)\,\otimes\,SO(\,5\,)
diagonal subgroup. The
H_{\beta\,{\bf{cd}}}
vielbein has nonzero both
H_{\beta\,{\bf{+b}}}
and also
H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
. The second vielbein is the term already in the matrix
{\mathbb{H}}
from the flat section (ultimate goal is to rewrite the
AdS
case in the terms of matrix
{\mathbb{H}}
and use the super-trace trick to get the pre-potential). The field
H_{\beta\,{\bf{+b}}}
is related to the
H_{\widetilde{\rho}\,{\bf{+c}}}
as we saw in table (
3
).

There is one last piece in the equation (
93
) that we did not relate to the fields in the
{\mathbb{H}}
matrix. The field
H_{\widetilde{\sigma}\,{\bf{a}}}
. As we saw in the flat case, that field should be related to
H_{\widetilde{\sigma}\,{\bf{+a}}}
via
P_{-}
. We have seen however (for example in (
37
)) that such relations are a bit changed in the
AdS
case.
Consider the following torsion constraint (and use mixed light-cone and
H_{-\,{\bf{+a}}}\,\rightsquigarrow\,0
):

\displaystyle T_{P\,\widetilde{D}\,S}\,\equiv\,T_{-\,\widetilde{\beta}\,{\bf{+a}}}\,=\,0\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,D_{[-}\,H_{\widetilde{\beta}\,{\bf{+a}})}\,+\,H_{[-\,|\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\widetilde{\beta}\,{\bf{+a}}){\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

(94)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,P_{-}\,H_{\widetilde{\beta}\,{\bf{+a}}}\,+\,H_{\widetilde{\beta}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{-\,{\bf{+a}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,+\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{-\,\widetilde{\beta}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,P_{-}\,H_{\widetilde{\beta}\,{\bf{+a}}}\,+\,\eta_{-\,+}\,H_{\widetilde{\beta}\,{\bf{a}}}\,+\,{\textstyle{1\over r_{AdS}}}\,(\gamma_{-})_{\beta\,\nu}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})^{\nu\,\sigma}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\nu}

(95)

In the table (
3
) we derived the relation between
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\nu}
and
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\nu}}
. That result together with (
95
) we get:

{\Big(}P_{-}\,-\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,{\textstyle{1\over(r_{AdS})^{2}}}{\Big)}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\alpha}}\,=\,H_{{\bf{a}}\,\widetilde{\alpha}}

(96)

With the equation (
96
) we succeeded to calculate the last missing derivative
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\sigma}}
in terms of
{\mathbb{H}}
vielbeins:

\displaystyle D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\sigma}}\,-\,2\,(\,P_{-}\,-{\textstyle{1\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,{\textstyle{1\over(r_{AdS})^{2}}}\,)\,(\gamma^{\bf{a}})_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\sigma}}\,

(97)

\displaystyle\,-\,{\textstyle{1\over 2\,(r_{AdS})^{2}\,P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\sigma}{}^{\nu}\,(\gamma^{\bf{d}})_{\nu\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\beta}{}^{\rho}\,H_{\widetilde{\rho}\,{\bf{+d}}}\,-\,{\textstyle{1\over 2\,(r_{AdS})}}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\sigma}{}^{\nu}\,(\gamma^{\bf{d}})_{\nu\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+d}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}\,H_{\widetilde{\beta}\,{\sigma}}\,-\,2\,(\,P_{\widetilde{-}}\,-\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{{-}}}}\,{\textstyle{1\over(r_{AdS})^{2}}}\,)\,(\gamma^{\bf{a}})_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta}\,H_{{\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}\,{\sigma}}\,

(98)

\displaystyle\,-\,{\textstyle{1\over 2\,(r_{AdS})^{2}\,P_{-}}}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\sigma}{}^{\nu}\,(\gamma^{\bf{d}})_{\nu\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\beta}{}^{\rho}\,H_{{\rho}\,\widetilde{\bf{+d}}}\,-\,{\textstyle{1\over 2\,(r_{AdS})}}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\sigma}{}^{\nu}\,(\gamma^{\bf{d}})_{\nu\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,H_{\widetilde{\beta}\,{\bf{+d}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

Where the
(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\sigma}{}^{\nu}\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,(\gamma^{+})_{\sigma\,\lambda}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})^{\lambda\,\nu}
. The
AdS
equations (
48
) and (
A.57
) and (
A.58
) and (
A.63
) and (
A.64
) and (
97
) and (
98
) could be summarised in the following diagram:

\textstyle{{(}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}},\,H_{\alpha\,\widetilde{\beta}}{)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{{(}H_{\beta\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}},\,H_{{\bf{+a}\,\widetilde{\beta}}}{)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{D_{{\alpha}^{\prime}}}

(99)

From the above diagram is obvious that we again have to combine
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
and
H_{\alpha\,\widetilde{\beta}}
and derivatives
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
and
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
to get a vanishing derivative on some scalar.

7.2
The
{\mathbb{H}}
matrix in
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}

We want to repeat the chapter on the flat solution via the
{\mathbb{H}}
matrix. The
{\mathbb{H}}
matrix was defined in (
83
). We want to write the action
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
on the
{\mathbb{H}}
. This was given in components in equations: (
48
) and (
A.57
) and (
A.58
) and (
A.63
) and (
A.64
) and (
97
) and (
98
) and also graphically in (
99
). We expect that the resulting matrix equations have pieces given by the flat equations (
69
) and (
71
) plus purely
AdS
pieces (dependent as powers of
{\textstyle{1\over r_{AdS}}}
). We could write those equations in such explicit matrix form, but resulting equations are complicated and unnecessary for our purpose. We instead summarise the right hand side of
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,{\mathbb{H}}
and
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}\,{\mathbb{H}}
using two new matrices
{\mathbb{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}
and
{\mathbb{Y}_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
respectively. We propose matrix from of the
AdS
equations:

\displaystyle D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}

(100)

\displaystyle D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}\,{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{Y}_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}}

(101)

The matrices
{\mathbb{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}
and
{\mathbb{Y}_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}}
are fully fixed by (
48
) and (
A.57
) and (
A.58
) and (
A.63
) and (
A.64
) and (
97
) and (
98
). In the
r_{AdS}\,\rightarrow\,\infty
the
{\mathbb{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}\,\rightarrow\,{\mathcal{\mathchar 256}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}\,{\mathbb{H}}
and
{\mathbb{Y}_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}}\,\rightarrow\,{\mathbb{H}}\,\mathchar 256_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
, where the matrices
\mathchar 256_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and
\mathchar 256_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
are given in (
69
) and (
71
).

7.3
Chiral and projective solutions for
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}

In the next step we repeat the argument we gave in the flat case section but for the
AdS
equations (
100
) and (
101
). We define:

{\mathcal{D}_{v}}\,\equiv\,{\mathcal{D}^{v}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,(\,D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,+\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}\,D_{\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}}\,)

(102)

now we act by (
102
) on
{\mathbb{H}}
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}^{v}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\,{\mathbb{X}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,+\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}\,{\mathbb{Y}}_{\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}}\,)

(103)

we multiply by
\mathbb{B}
and apply
Str
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}^{v}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,\mbox{Str}\,(\,{\mathbb{H}}\,{\mathbb{B}}\,)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{Str}\,{\big(}(\,{\mathbb{X}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,+\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}\,{\mathbb{Y}}_{\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}}\,)\,{\mathbb{B}}\,{\big)}

(104)

We will further analyse the structure of (
104
) in next discussion but before we note one change with respect to (
73
). In (
73
) we used
{\mathbb{B}}^{-1}
here we are using (yet to be determined) matrix
{\mathbb{B}}
, the difference is purely conventional. As in the flat case, we want to put the right hand side of (
104
) to zero and by that obtain vanishing
{\mathcal{D}}^{v}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
on some scalar field
\mbox{Str}\,(\,{\mathbb{H}}\,{\mathbb{B}}\,)
, that will be called pre-potential. In the flat space it was crucial that we had the identity (
79
). It was used in the relation (
74
). Similarly in the
AdS
case the identity (
79
) will also be crucial.

In the solution of the vanishing (
104
) right hand side we still want to maintain the
SO\,(\,4\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,4\,)
invariance. Therefore the
\mathbb{B}
matrix has a block-diagonal form:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{B}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\begin{pmatrix}b_{{\bf{+a}}\,{\bf{+b}}}&0\\
0&b_{\alpha\,\beta}\\
\end{pmatrix}

(105)

Let us also simplify the notation for the constants appearing in the equations (
48
) and (
97
) and similarly for their left-right conjugates. In (
48
) we redefine:

\displaystyle X_{1}\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,-\,{\textstyle{1\over g}}\,{\textstyle{1\over(r_{AdS})\,P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,\,\,

\displaystyle||

\displaystyle\,\,\,X_{2}\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,{\textstyle{1\over 2\,g}}\,(\,1\,-\,{\textstyle{1\over f}}\,{\textstyle{1\over(r_{AdS})^{2}\,P_{-}\,P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,)

(106)

\displaystyle X_{3}\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,+\,{\textstyle{1\over f\,g}}\,{\textstyle{1\over 2\,(r_{AdS})^{3}\,P_{-}\,(P_{\widetilde{-}})^{2}}}

\displaystyle||

where the
f
and
g
were defined in (
49
). In (
97
) we define:

\displaystyle Y_{1}\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,-\,2\,{\Big(}\,P_{\widetilde{-}}\,-\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{-}}}\,{\textstyle{1\over(r_{AdS})^{2}}}\,{\Big)}\,\,\,||\,\,\,Y_{2}\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,-\,{\textstyle{1\over 2\,(r_{AdS})^{2}\,P_{-}}}\,\,\,||\,\,\,Y_{3}\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,-\,{\textstyle{1\over 2\,(r_{AdS})}}

(107)

With the definitions (
105
), (
106
) and (
107
) let us rewrite the right hand side of (

) explicitly:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\,X_{1}\,-\,X_{3}\,)\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})^{\beta\,\sigma}\,b_{{\bf{+a}}\,{\bf{+b}}}\,(\gamma^{{\bf{b}}})_{\sigma\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,-\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\sigma^{\prime}}\,X_{2}\,b_{{\bf{+a}}\,{\bf{+b}}}\,(\gamma^{{\bf{b}}})^{\beta}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}

(108)

\displaystyle+\,Y_{1}\,b^{\beta\,\sigma}\,(\gamma_{{\bf{a}}})_{\sigma\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,+\,Y_{2}\,b^{\nu\,\sigma}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\sigma}{}^{\lambda}\,(\gamma_{{\bf{a}}})_{\lambda\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\nu}{}^{\beta}\,+\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\sigma^{\prime}}\,Y_{3}\,b^{\beta\,\sigma}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\sigma}{}^{\lambda}\,(\gamma_{{\bf{a}}})_{\lambda\,\sigma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\nu^{\prime}}\,(\,\tilde{X}_{1}\,-\,\tilde{X}_{3}\,)\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})^{\beta\,\sigma}\,b_{{\bf{+a}}\,{\bf{+b}}}\,(\gamma^{{\bf{b}}})_{\sigma\,\nu^{\prime}}\,-\,X_{2}\,b_{{\bf{+a}}\,{\bf{+b}}}\,(\gamma^{{\bf{b}}})^{\beta}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}

(109)

\displaystyle+\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\nu^{\prime}}\,\tilde{Y}_{1}\,b^{\beta\,\sigma}\,(\gamma_{{\bf{a}}})_{\sigma\,\nu^{\prime}}\,+\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\nu^{\prime}}\,\tilde{Y}_{2}\,b^{\nu\,\sigma}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\sigma}{}^{\lambda}\,(\gamma_{{\bf{a}}})_{\lambda\,\nu^{\prime}}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\nu}{}^{\beta}\,+\,{Y}_{3}\,b^{\beta\,\sigma}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\sigma}{}^{\lambda}\,(\gamma_{{\bf{a}}})_{\lambda\,\alpha^{\prime}}

where
\tilde{X}_{1}
,
\tilde{X_{3}}
and
\tilde{Y}_{1}
,

) and
X_{2}
and

The equations (
108
) and (
109
) are the
AdS
analogies of the flat space equations (
92
). To solve them we first multiply the equation (
109
) by matrix
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}
. Thus we get the equation (
109
) into the form:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}^{2}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\nu^{\prime}}\,(\,\tilde{X}_{1}\,-\,\tilde{X}_{3}\,)\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})^{\beta\,\sigma}\,b_{{\bf{+a}}\,{\bf{+b}}}\,(\gamma^{{\bf{b}}})_{\sigma\,\nu^{\prime}}\,-\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\nu^{\prime}}\,X_{2}\,b_{{\bf{+a}}\,{\bf{+b}}}\,(\gamma^{{\bf{b}}})^{\beta}{}_{\nu^{\prime}}

(110)

\displaystyle+\,{\mathcal{M}}^{2}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\nu^{\prime}}\,\tilde{Y}_{1}\,b^{\beta\,\sigma}\,(\gamma_{{\bf{a}}})_{\sigma\,\nu^{\prime}}\,+\,{\mathcal{M}}^{2}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\nu^{\prime}}\,\tilde{Y}_{2}\,b^{\nu\,\sigma}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\sigma}{}^{\lambda}\,(\gamma_{{\bf{a}}})_{\lambda\,\nu^{\prime}}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\nu}{}^{\beta}

\displaystyle+\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\nu^{\prime}}\,{Y}_{3}\,b^{\beta\,\sigma}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\sigma}{}^{\lambda}\,(\gamma_{{\bf{a}}})_{\lambda\,\nu^{\prime}}

The equation (
110
) is almost identical to the (
108
) except of the left-right swapped constants and
{\mathcal{M}}^{2}
matrix. By suitable choice of the
{\mathcal{M}}
matrix we can turn (
110
) into (
108
) and thus reduce number of equations by half. By that we get the condition on the matrix
{\mathcal{M}}
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}^{2}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}\,=\,q^{2}\,\delta_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}

(111)

where the constant
q^{2}\,=\,{\textstyle{P_{-}\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}
. By that choice of the matrix
{\mathcal{M}}^{2}
and constant
q^{2}
we turn equation (
110
) into (
108
). Furthermore we should solve relation (
111
) for the matrix
{\mathcal{M}}
. As in the whole
AdS
section we ask for the
SO\,(\,4\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,4\,)
invariance. With that requirement we get two branches for the
{\mathcal{M}}
matrix (actually we get four, as we will see, but the
\pm
is not very important to us):

\displaystyle({\mathcal{M}}^{2})_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}\,=\,{\textstyle{P_{{-}}\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,\delta_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}\,\,\,\,\Rightarrow\,\,\,\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}\,=\,\pm\,\sqrt{{\textstyle{P_{{-}}\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}}\,\begin{cases}\delta_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}&\\
(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}&\end{cases}

(112)

We first notice few nice properties of (
112
). The solution is actually the same as in the flat case, see (
91
). We are in the
AdS
space but the matrix
{\mathcal{M}}
that combines
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
does not depend on the
r_{AdS}
. Unfortunately we got the same not very nice square root factor in (
112
). We would need to find some way to deal with it.

Having solved one half of equations (
108
) and (
109
). We solve the second half, that is just relation (
108
):

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\,X_{1}\,-\,X_{3}\,)\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})^{\beta\,\sigma}\,b_{{\bf{+a}}\,{\bf{+b}}}\,(\gamma^{{\bf{b}}})_{\sigma\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,-\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\sigma^{\prime}}\,X_{2}\,b_{{\bf{+a}}\,{\bf{+b}}}\,(\gamma^{{\bf{b}}})^{\beta}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}

(113)

\displaystyle+\,Y_{1}\,b^{\beta\,\sigma}\,(\gamma_{{\bf{a}}})_{\sigma\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,+\,Y_{2}\,b^{\nu\,\sigma}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\sigma}{}^{\lambda}\,(\gamma_{{\bf{a}}})_{\lambda\,\alpha^{\prime}}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\nu}{}^{\beta}\,+\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\sigma^{\prime}}\,Y_{3}\,b^{\beta\,\sigma}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\sigma}{}^{\lambda}\,(\gamma_{{\bf{a}}})_{\lambda\,\sigma^{\prime}}

The claim is that given solution
{\mathcal{M}}
the block matrices
b_{\bf{+a}\,{\bf{+b}}}
and
b_{\alpha\,\beta}
are fixed (up to the overall constant). We will again use the same identity (
79
) as in the flat case. We expect the solutions (we have two branches) will be certain
r_{AdS}
dependent deformation of the original flat space solutions. We also require to maintain the
SO(\,4\,)\,\otimes\,SO\,(\,4\,)
invariance of the solution so the most general ansatz for the equation (
113
) is:

\displaystyle b_{{\bf{+a}}\,{\bf{+b}}}\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,{A}\,\delta_{{\bf{a}}\,{\bf{b}}}\,+\,B\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{{\bf{a}}\,{\bf{b}}}\,\,\,\,||\,\,\,\,b_{\alpha\,\beta}\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,C\,\delta_{\alpha\,\beta}\,+\,D\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\alpha\,\beta}

(114)

Because of later importance we will first solve the
(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})
branch of the
{\mathcal{M}}
solution (
112
). Later we will also provide solution for the
\delta
branch of the (
112
). We plug
{\mathcal{M}}
and (
114
) into (
113
) and solve for
A,\,\,B,\,\,C\,\,\mbox{and}\,\,D
using the identity (
79
), we remind that
q\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,\pm\,\sqrt{{\textstyle{P_{{-}}\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}}
. We get the following solutions:

the
(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}
branch:

(115)

\displaystyle det\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,{\big(}\,(\,X_{1}\,-\,X_{3}\,)^{2}\,-\,(\,q\,X_{2}\,)^{2}\,{\big)}

\displaystyle||

\displaystyle A\,=\,{\textstyle{D\over det}}\,{\big(}\,(\,X_{1}\,-\,X_{3}\,)\,(\,Y_{1}\,+Y_{2}\,)\,+\,q^{2}\,X_{2}\,Y_{3}\,{\big)}

\displaystyle||

\displaystyle B\,=\,q\,{\textstyle{D\over det}}\,{\big(}\,X_{2}\,(\,Y_{1}\,+\,Y_{2}\,)\,+\,(X_{1}\,-\,X_{3})\,Y_{3}\,{\big)}

\displaystyle C\,=\,0

\displaystyle||

the
\delta_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}
branch:

(116)

\displaystyle det\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,{\big(}\,(\,Y_{1}\,+\,Y_{2}\,)^{2}\,-\,(\,q\,Y_{3}\,)^{2}\,{\big)}

\displaystyle||

\displaystyle||

\displaystyle B\,=\,0

\displaystyle C\,=\,q\,{\textstyle{A\over det}}\,{\big(}\,(\,Y_{1}\,+\,Y_{2}\,)\,X_{2}\,+\,(\,X_{1}\,-\,X_{3}\,)\,Y_{3}\,{\big)}

\displaystyle||

\displaystyle D\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,-\,{\textstyle{A\over det}}\,{\big(}\,(Y_{1}\,+\,Y_{2}\,)\,(\,X_{1}\,-\,X_{3}\,)\,+\,q^{2}\,X_{2}\,Y_{3}\,{\big)}

We can again see that as we do
r_{AdS}\,\rightarrow\,\infty
limit in (
115
) we will get the flat solution (
91
), keeping the
D
( or
A
in
\delta
branch )
r_{AdS}
independent in that limit.

7.4
Near horizon limit

In the previous section we found the structure of the linearised pre-potential (
115
) and (
116
) and also the construction of
{\mathcal{D}^{v}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}
that vanishes on the pre-potential (
102
) and (
112
). We will now introduce the complementary derivative
{\mathcal{D}}^{w}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
that is constructed after picking the
{\mathcal{D}^{v}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}
derivative (i.e. picking the matrix
{\mathcal{M}}
in (
112
)) and changing the sign in front of the
{\mathcal{M}}
(the second linearly independent combination). Thus we have:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}^{w}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,-\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}\,D_{\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}}

(117)

The notation for the upper indices
v
and
w
in ((
102
) and (
117
)) comes from equivalent notation for
d_{v}
and
d_{w}
derivatives used in
[
7
]
, (and also for
d_{u}
and
d_{\bar{u}}
, whose analogies are to be defined later). In analogy with the paper
[
7
]
we want to define the
{\mathcal{P}}_{+}
operator that has
{\mathcal{D}}^{v}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and
{\mathcal{D}}^{w}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
as eigenvectors with nonzero eigenvalues. We can solve for
{\mathcal{P}}_{+}
in full generality, i.e. keeping the non-local square root factors in derivatives
{\mathcal{D}}^{v}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and
{\mathcal{D}}^{w}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
. This would introduce the non-local square root factors also into the definition of
{\mathcal{P}}_{+}
and would cause further problems. What we will do instead is to restrict the coordinate dependence of the pre-potential
V
to be just the
PSU\,(2,\,2\,|\,4)
. This is the same algebra we wanted to use at the beginning of this project, but we were forced to extend it to the full
SO\,(10)\,\otimes\,SO\,(10)
T-dually extended super-algebra. Now, we want to restrict just the coordinate dependence of the pre-potential. Doing so the
P_{-}\,=\,P_{\widetilde{-}}

on
pre-potential, not everywhere. That is enough to get rid of the non-local factors in
{\mathcal{D}}^{v}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and
{\mathcal{D}}^{w}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
as they act on pre-potential. Then we can redefine (
102
) and (
117
) by saying that the new square root free
{\mathcal{D}}^{v}
and
{\mathcal{D}}^{w}
to be our new definitions. With this it is easy to see that the good definition of
{\mathcal{P}}_{+}
is:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}_{+}\,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,(\,P_{+}\,+\,P_{\widetilde{+}}\,)\,=\,P_{+}

(118)

where the last equality holds on pre-potential.

Following the definitions in
[
7
]
of the
AdS
boundary limit we propose that any operator
{\mathcal{K}}
which is an eigenvector of
{\mathcal{P}}_{+}
operator, i.e.
[\,{\mathcal{P}}_{+},\,{\mathcal{K}}\,]\,=\,{\bf{c}}\,{\mathcal{K}}
, scales as
R^{\bf{c}}
as we approach the boundary, i.e.
R\,\rightarrow\,0
limit, where
R
is a radial coordinate on the Poincaré patch. Another way how to state the limit is that by putting the
R\,\rightarrow\,0
we contract the isometry groups
SO\,(\,4,\,1\,)
and
SO\,(\,4,\,1\,)
to
ISO\,(\,3,1\,)
and
ISO\,(\,3,1\,)
(we Wick rotated the
S^{5}
isometry group for the purpose of this limit). For more details on this limit (that can be stated also through the explicit coordinates on
AdS_{5}
and
S^{5}
) see notes
[
7
]
.

Using the previous definitions of the
AdS
boundary limit we can analyse the different branches of the
{\mathcal{D}}^{v}
solutions (
112
). Let’s first pick the
\delta_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}
branch (let’s work with both
\pm
sub-branches at once). Note that even on pre-potential the
D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,\neq\,D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
as can be seen from the explicit construction of those derivatives in
[
24
]
in the section
{\bf{5}}
. Then the commutator is:

\displaystyle[\,{\mathcal{P}}_{+},\,D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,+\,D_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}]\,=\,\pm\,{\textstyle{1\over r_{AdS}}}\,(\gamma_{+})_{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta^{\prime}}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})^{\beta^{\prime}\,\sigma^{\prime}}\,(D_{\sigma^{\prime}}\,\pm\,D_{\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}})\,+\,\dots

(119)

The
\dots
part correspond to the current that vanishes in the super-gravity limit (i.e. we do not see string parameter
\sigma
) and on pre-potential. We also used the commutators from (
2
) and the mixed
AdS
commutators from (
23
). We also used the explicit solution for the
PSU\,(\,2,\,2\,|\,4\,)
(we are on pre-potential) derivatives in terms of
\tau
and
\sigma
currents, see section
{\bf{5}}
in
[
24
]
. More specifically we used that
D_{\mathchar 266}\,\equiv\,D^{\alpha^{\prime}}\,=\,\omega^{\alpha^{\prime}}\,+\,{\textstyle{1\over 2}}\,{\textstyle{1\over r_{AdS}}}\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})^{\alpha^{\prime}\,\beta^{\prime}}\,D_{\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime}}
, where the
\omega^{\alpha^{\prime}}
is the current proportional to
\sigma
derivative and it has to vanish in the super-gravity limit. The equation (
119
) is very interesting. It tells us how the
{\mathcal{D}}^{v}
scales for the
\pm

\delta_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}
branch of (
112
). We also notice that the scaling constant is
r_{AdS}
dependent and vanishes for
r_{AdS}\,\rightarrow\,\infty
. More importantly because of the
(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})
for fixed
r_{AdS}
and for fixed sub-branch of
\pm\,\delta_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}
the scaling constant
{\bf{c}}
is either
+\,({\textstyle{1\over r_{AdS}}})
for one half of
SO\,(8)
chiral index
\alpha^{\prime}
or
-\,({\textstyle{1\over r_{AdS}}})
for second half. And this is not good because by
[
7
]
the
{\mathcal{D}}^{v}
derivative should scale like
{\textstyle{1\over R}}
and
{\mathcal{D}}^{w}
should scale like
R
(put
r_{AdS}\,=\,1
for simplicity). In (
119
) we can see that just
{\textstyle{1\over 2}}
of derivatives scale properly. This boundary limit then distinguishes between two branches of (
112
). In the following we will see that the
(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})
branch has exactly right scaling properties so it corresponds to the right solution. Without this boundary limit we did not have a way how to pick a branch in (
112
). In the case of
(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})
branch we have one more
(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})
matrix in (
119
) thus we get:

\displaystyle[\,{\mathcal{P}}_{+},\,D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,\pm\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\rho^{\prime}}\,D_{\widetilde{\rho}^{\prime}}]\,=\,\pm\,{\textstyle{1\over r_{AdS}}}\,(D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,\pm\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\rho^{\prime}}\,D_{\widetilde{\rho}^{\prime}})\,+\,\dots

(120)

The equation (
120
) will give us the correct solution. From (
120
) we can see that for fixed
r_{AdS}
and for fixed
(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})
sub-branch we will have proper scaling for full
SO\,(\,8\,)
chiral index
\alpha^{\prime}
. Because we require
{\mathcal{D}}^{v}
to scale like
{\textstyle{1\over R}}
and
{\mathcal{D}}^{w}
scale like
R
we have
{\mathcal{D}}^{v}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,=\,(D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,-\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\rho^{\prime}}\,D_{\widetilde{\rho}^{\prime}})
and
{\mathcal{D}}^{w}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,=\,(D_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,+\,(\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5})_{\alpha^{\prime}}{}^{\rho^{\prime}}\,D_{\widetilde{\rho}^{\prime}})
. The positive news is that the blowing-up derivative
{\mathcal{D}}^{v}
is zero on the pre-potential by our construction, so there is no possible singularity arising as we approach the boundary.

Its easy to see how the derivatives
D_{\alpha}
and
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}}
scale. Because the
(\gamma_{+})_{\alpha\,\beta}\,=\,0
the
[\,{\mathcal{P}}_{+},\,D_{\alpha}\,]\,=\,[\,{\mathcal{P}}_{+},\,D_{\widetilde{\alpha}}\,]\,=\,0
. So they scale like
1
. Those derivatives are building up the
{\mathcal{D}}^{u}
and
{\mathcal{D}}^{\bar{u}}
, analogous derivatives to paper
[
7
]
derivatives
d_{u}
and
d_{\bar{u}}
. The explicit forms of
{\mathcal{D}}^{u}
and
{\mathcal{D}}^{\bar{u}}
won’t be needed in this paper so we do not provide them.

7.5
Near horizon limit and field equations

Comparing result with
[
7
]
we want to see that the field equations for the pre-potential in the near horizon limit (i.e. in the
R\,\rightarrow\,0
) is just of the form
{\mathcal{P}}_{+}\,V\,=\,0\,+\,{\mathcal{O}}(\,R\,)
. This will be our final confirmation that we discovered the right pre-potential. We first notice that the Lorentz generator scales like
{\mathcal{O}}(\,1\,)
, this can be seen from commutator
[\,S_{\bf{+a}},\,{\mathcal{P}}_{+}\,]\,=\,[\,S_{\widetilde{\bf{+a}}},\,{\mathcal{P}}_{+}\,]\,=\,0
. To see what is
{\mathcal{P}}_{+}
on pre-potential we could directly use some appropriate torsions (remember pre-potential is a linear combination of fields). We found it easier however to use a different approach. Let’s look at the torsion constraint (
38
) but for the
\alpha
index instead of
\alpha^{\prime}
(the
\alpha
index is one of the
SO\,(8)
chiral indices):

\displaystyle T_{D\,S\,\widetilde{S}}\,\equiv\,T_{\alpha\,{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,=\,0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{[\alpha}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}})}\,+\,H_{[\alpha\,|\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\,{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}})\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}

(121)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,+\,S_{\widetilde{+b}}\,H_{\alpha\,{\bf{+a}}}\,+\,S_{\bf{+a}}H_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,\alpha}

(122)

First notice that the structure of (
122
) is very different than the structure of (
38
). There is no
f
term in (
122
) and there is the full derivative term present. Even in the
AdS
case the
f
term is missing. This can be seen as follows. The
f_{\bf{+a}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,=\,0
in
AdS
and also in flat case and also
f_{\alpha\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,=\,0
. The only possibly nonzero
f
term is coming from
f_{\alpha\,{\bf{+a}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}
. The
H_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,f_{\alpha\,{\bf{+a}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,\propto\,(\gamma_{\bf{+a}})_{\alpha}{}^{\nu^{\prime}}\,H_{\nu^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
. The vielbein
H_{\nu^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
is zero (also in the
AdS
) as was shown in the analysis under (
38
).
Next, we can recognise the term
H_{\alpha\,\widetilde{\bf{+a}}}
as a part of
{\mathbb{H}}
matrix (
68
). The vielbein
H_{\alpha\,{\bf{+a}}}
has also been analysed in table (
3
). It is related to
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\alpha}}
, see table (
3
). We need to be more careful with that relation because in (
122
) we again discover the
S
derivative peculiarity, we saw earlier.

In general all fields in
{\mathbb{H}}
(now better viewed as their irreducible pieces) could be obtained from the pre-potential
V
by an action of appropriate (irreducible) combination of
{\mathcal{D}}^{w}
on the pre-potential. One could analyse in full detail what is the exact structure of those pieces and reproduce famous field content of
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
supergravity first discovered in
[
2
]
and later used in
[
3
]
. This would lead us away from this paper real aim, so we postpone this analysis to next paper. The aim of this section is to show that on pre-potential
V
the operator
{\mathcal{P}}_{+}
vanishes in the near horizon limit.

For this reason we notice following expansions:

\displaystyle H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\bf{c}}_{0}\,V\,+\,{\bf{c}}_{2}\,({\mathcal{D}}^{w})^{2}\,V\,+\,{\bf{c}}_{4}\,({\mathcal{D}}^{w})^{4}\,V\,+\,{\bf{c}}_{6}\,({\mathcal{D}}^{w})^{6}\,V\,+\,{\bf{c}}_{8}\,({\mathcal{D}}^{w})^{8}\,V

(123)

\displaystyle H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\bf{d}}_{1}\,{\mathcal{D}}^{w}\,V\,+\,{\bf{d}}_{3}\,({\mathcal{D}}^{w})^{3}\,V\,+\,{\bf{d}}_{5}\,({\mathcal{D}}^{w})^{5}\,V\,+\,{\bf{d}}_{7}\,({\mathcal{D}}^{w})^{7}\,V

(124)

\displaystyle H_{\widetilde{\bf{+a}}\,\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\bf{e}}_{1}\,{\mathcal{D}}^{w}\,V\,+\,{\bf{e}}_{3}\,({\mathcal{D}}^{w})^{3}\,V\,+\,{\bf{e}}_{5}\,({\mathcal{D}}^{w})^{5}\,V\,+\,{\bf{e}}_{7}\,({\mathcal{D}}^{w})^{7}\,V

(125)

where factors
{\bf{c}_{0}},\,{\bf{c}_{2}}\,\dots,\,{\bf{d}_{1}},\,{\bf{d}_{3}}\,\dots
and
{\bf{e}_{1}},\,{\bf{e}_{3}}\,\dots
are constant factors with appropriate index structure. Note that the
{\bf{c}_{0}}
is non-zero. The important observation is that for each term in (
123
), (
124
) and (
125
) we know how it scales in the
R\,\rightarrow\,0
limit, because we know that
{\mathcal{D}}^{w}
scales like
R
.

Next, we want to combine (
122
) with known scalings of all (
122
) objects to get an information how
D_{\alpha}\,V
scales. On one hand it should scale like
{\mathcal{O}}\,(1)
on the other hand the relation (
122
) relates it to different fields. What we obtain is a nontrivial relation that
D_{\alpha}\,V\,=\,{\mathcal{O}}\,(\,R\,)
as we go to the boundary. It just means that
D_{\alpha}\,V\,=\,0
(and so also
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}}\,V\,=\,0
) in the near horizon limit. Because of the anti-commutator
\{\,D_{\alpha},\,D_{\beta}\}\,=\,2\,(\gamma_{-})_{\alpha\,\beta}\,P_{+}
. This is enough to see that
P_{+}\,V\,\equiv\,{\mathcal{P}}_{+}\,V\,=\,0
in the near horizon limit. There are two crucial steps. One is to relate the (
122
) term
S_{\widetilde{{\bf{+b}}}}\,H_{\alpha\,{\bf{+a}}}
to
H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\alpha}}
. This is relatively straightforward using table (
3
) and explicit
S_{\widetilde{{\bf{+b}}}}
derivative. Second step is to plug expansions (
123
), (
124
) and (
125
) and the scalings of particular pieces into (
122
). Doing that we get the following:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,+\,S_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,H_{\alpha\,{\bf{+a}}}\,+\,S_{\bf{+a}}\,H_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}\,\alpha}

(126)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}\,H_{\bf{+a}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,+\,S_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,{\Big(}\,-\,f_{\widetilde{-}\,\alpha\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}}\,\eta^{{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{M}$}}}}{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,{\mbox{{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{N}$}}}}}\,{\Big)}\,+\,S_{\bf{+a}}\,H_{\widetilde{{\bf{+b}}}\,\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}\,H_{\bf{+a}\,\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}\,-\,{\textstyle{{\bf{c}}\over r_{AdS}}}\,(\gamma_{-})_{\alpha\,\nu}\,(\,\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5}\,)^{\nu\,\sigma}\,S_{\widetilde{{\bf{+b}}}}\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,H_{{\bf{+a}}\,\widetilde{\sigma}}\,+\,S_{\bf{+a}}\,H_{\widetilde{{\bf{+b}}}\,\alpha}

(127)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}\,{\Big(}\,{\bf{c}}_{0}\,V\,+\,{\bf{c}}_{2}\,({\mathcal{D}}^{w})^{2}\,V\,+\,\dots\,{\Big)}

(128)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,{\textstyle{1\over r_{AdS}}}\,(\gamma_{-})_{\alpha\,\nu}\,(\,\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5}\,)^{\nu\,\sigma}\,S_{\widetilde{{\bf{+b}}}}\,{\textstyle{1\over P_{\widetilde{-}}}}\,{\Big(}\,{\bf{d}}_{1}\,{\mathcal{D}}^{w}\,V\,+\,{\bf{d}}_{3}\,({\mathcal{D}}^{w})^{3}\,V\,+\,\dots\,{\Big)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle S_{\bf{+a}}\,{\Big(}\,{\bf{e}}_{1}\,{\mathcal{D}}^{w}\,V\,+\,{\bf{e}}_{3}\,({\mathcal{D}}^{w})^{3}\,V\,+\,\dots\,{\Big)}\,

The equation (
127
) contains all the right expressions to establish the near horizon limit. By the discussion below (
120
) the
{\mathcal{D}}^{w}{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
derivative scales like
{\mathcal{O}}\,(\,R\,)
(for the projective branch), we also have the scaling of
S_{\bf{+a}}
and
S_{\widetilde{\bf{+b}}}
that goes like a constant. Applying that knowledge we get the equation (
127
) in the near horizon limit:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\bf{c}}_{0}\,D_{\alpha}\,V\,+\,{\mathcal{O}}\,(R)\,

(129)

The
{\bf{c}_{0}}
is nonzero constant (tensor) so it follows that
D_{\alpha}\,V\,=\,0
at the
AdS
boundary. From
\{\,D_{\alpha},\,D_{\beta}\,\}\,=\,(\gamma_{-})_{\alpha\,\beta}\,P_{+}
we get the field equation for the pre-potential in the near horizon limit:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{+}V\,+\,{\mathcal{O}}\,(R)

(130)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}_{+}V\,+\,{\mathcal{O}}\,(R)

(131)

8
Conclusion

We outline results we have obtained: starting from the
10
dimensional IIB string theory. We embedded the
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
background and expanded the theory around this background (we also considered a flat background, i.e.
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
with
r_{AdS}\,\rightarrow\,\infty
). Our aim was to obtain (linearised) pre-potential with desired properties in the case of
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
(also in the flat case). We succeeded and obtained pre-potential construction for flat and
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
background. We derived only the linearised form, but the vielbein construction makes non-linearisation straightforward perturbation. The pre-potential (in flat and also in
AdS_{5}\,\times\,S^{5}
, the projective and chiral) sits in the combination (without further derivatives) of vielbeins
H_{S\,\widetilde{S}}
and
H_{D\,\widetilde{D}}
. By construction the
D^{v}
derivative vanishes in bulk on the pre-potential and the (projective) pre-potential satisfies the near horizon limit field equation
{\mathcal{P}}_{+}\,V\,=\,0\,+\,{\mathcal{O}}\,(R)
together with vanishing of
{\mathcal{D}}^{u}
and
{\mathcal{D}}^{\bar{u}}
on pre-potential in the near horizon limit. This near horizon limit picks out the projective pre-potential instead of chiral pre-potential (both were obtained as valid bulk solutions).

The vanishing of
{\mathcal{P}}_{+}
at the boundary fixes the difference between the conformal weights (
\equiv\,\mathchar 257
) and
U(1)
charges (
\equiv\,\mathchar 257_{Y}
) of all boundary BPS operators, since
{\mathcal{P}}_{+}\,\propto\,\mathchar 257\,-\,\mathchar 257_{Y}
. The
{\mathcal{P}}_{-}\,\propto\,\mathchar 257\,+\,\mathchar 257_{Y}
and known expansion of
{\mathbb{H}}
in powers of
{\mathcal{D}}^{w}
from
V
, fixes the conformal weights and the
U(1)
charges for the boundary BPS operators, the relations important in the
AdS/CFT
correspondence, see
[
4
]
,
[
5
]
.

Note that in the lines (

of the diagonal subgroup given

. The
\pm
then comes from the fact that by the construction
\widetilde{\mathchar 256}_{5}
commutes with
{\bf{a}}\,\in\,\{\,10,\,1\,\dots\,4\,\}
and anti-commutes with
{\bf{a}}\,\in\,\{\,5,\dots\,9\,\}
. We used the prior definition in this section for some convenience. In the final expressions we will always use the definition without the
\pm
